### Diff-Palm: Realistic Palmprint Generation with Polynomial Creases and Intra-Class Variation Controllable Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18312v1)
- **Authors**: Jianlong Jin, Chenglong Zhao, Ruixin Zhang, Sheng Shang, Jianqing Xu, Jingyun Zhang, ShaoMing Wang, Yang Zhao, Shouhong Ding, Wei Jia, Yunsheng Wu
- **Abstract**: Palmprint recognition is significantly limited by the lack of large-scale publicly available datasets. Previous methods have adopted B\'ezier curves to simulate the palm creases, which then serve as input for conditional GANs to generate realistic palmprints. However, without employing real data fine-tuning, the performance of the recognition model trained on these synthetic datasets would drastically decline, indicating a large gap between generated and real palmprints. This is primarily due to the utilization of an inaccurate palm crease representation and challenges in balancing intra-class variation with identity consistency. To address this, we introduce a polynomial-based palm crease representation that provides a new palm crease generation mechanism more closely aligned with the real distribution. We also propose the palm creases conditioned diffusion model with a novel intra-class variation control method. By applying our proposed $K$-step noise-sharing sampling, we are able to synthesize palmprint datasets with large intra-class variation and high identity consistency. Experimental results show that, for the first time, recognition models trained solely on our synthetic datasets, without any fine-tuning, outperform those trained on real datasets. Furthermore, our approach achieves superior recognition performance as the number of generated identities increases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Diff-Palm: Realistic Palmprint Generation with Polynomial Creases and Intra-Class Variation Controllable Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating realistic palmprint datasets for improving palmprint recognition. The core contributions are:

1.  **Polynomial Crease Representation:**  Instead of Bézier curves, the authors propose representing palm creases using polynomial curves.  This allows for a more accurate and expressive representation of real palm creases by fitting curves to manually labeled points and estimating a multivariate Gaussian distribution for curve parameters.
2.  **Intra-Class Variation Controllable Diffusion Model:** They introduce a diffusion model conditioned on these polynomial creases. A novel *K-step noise-sharing sampling* technique is used to control the intra-class variation of the generated palmprints, preserving identity consistency. This involves sharing the noise added during certain steps of the denoising process across samples of the same identity.
3.  **Superior Performance:** The authors claim that recognition models trained *solely* on their synthetically generated data outperform models trained on real datasets, and without requiring any fine-tuning.
4. * Extensive experimental results* provide empirical evidence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Polynomial Crease Representation:** Replacing Bezier curves with Polynomial curves, along with a data-driven sampling mechanism is novel and makes theoretical sense given real palm crease complexity, and improves the match to real data, as the experiments show.
    *   **K-step Noise Sharing:** The idea of controlling intra-class variation through noise sharing in diffusion models is a notable contribution. The insight that applying noise-sharing at different stages of the denoising process affects style vs. crease detail consistency is interesting.
    *   **Outperforming Real Data:** The bold claim of surpassing performance of models trained on real data is *potentially* groundbreaking, but requires careful scrutiny (see below).

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing Data Scarcity:** Palmprint recognition suffers from a lack of publicly available datasets.  This work directly addresses this limitation.
    *   **Improved Recognition:** If the results hold up, this technique can produce synthetic training data that can be an effective proxy for real data, reducing or eliminating the need for large, costly real-data collection efforts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:**  The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of previous palmprint generation methods.
    *   **Sound Methodology:** The approach is well-motivated and technically sound, combining a more accurate palm crease model with a controllable diffusion model.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes extensive experiments, including ablation studies and comparisons against other methods and real data. They use multiple metrics to evaluate various aspects of generated data, including its diversity, uniqueness, and identity consistency.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper offers a good analysis of the results and explains the effects of the different components (e.g., polynomial creases, K-step noise sharing).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained PCEM:** The model requires a pre-trained PCEM model to extract the condition images. The performance could vary based on quality of extraction of the PCEM model.

    *   **Choice of Public Datasets:** While they use several public datasets, the age, quality, and acquisition methods of these datasets vary.  It's not entirely clear if outperforming these specific datasets translates to generalizable improvements. More public datasets should be incorporated.

    *   **Limited Real Data Volume:**  The "real data" baseline might not be as strong as it could be. It has 29.3k images with 2.2k identities. The synthesized datasets range up to 1.2M images generated with 60k identities. The authors do not report any computational cost involved in achieving the improved results, so the potential cost advantages are not explored.

    *   **Overly Optimistic Claim:**  The claim that models trained *solely* on their synthetic data outperform those trained on real data needs to be tempered. It's possible their synthetic data is particularly well-suited to the task (and perhaps, in some ways, *too* perfect), or that there are subtle biases introduced by the generation process. The comparison benchmarks may also influence this claim. Further investigation to validate this finding is required.
    *   **Dataset Release:** While they state they will release URLs to the anonymous images, due to the privacy risks with hand images this might not be possible or viable for all researchers. This is a common problem with generative model research.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field if the results are reproducible and generalizable. The proposed techniques for generating realistic and controllable palmprint data could be valuable for developing more robust palmprint recognition systems. Furthermore, the K-step noise sharing idea might be applicable to other biometric data generation tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has several strengths, including a novel approach, well-designed experiments, and detailed analysis. The polynomial representation for palm creases and the K-step noise sharing for controlling intra-class variation are creative and potentially impactful. However, the over-optimistic claims about outperforming real data, the choice of evaluation datasets, and practical limitations due to the difficulty of real dataset acquisition, prevent a higher score. Therefore, I would assign the paper a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeepFund: Will LLM be Professional at Fund Investment? A Live Arena Perspective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18313v1)
- **Authors**: Changlun Li, Yao Shi, Yuyu Luo, Nan Tang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across various domains, but their effectiveness in financial decision making, particularly in fund investment, remains inadequately evaluated. Current benchmarks primarily assess LLMs understanding of financial documents rather than their ability to manage assets or analyze trading opportunities in dynamic market conditions. A critical limitation in existing evaluation methodologies is the backtesting approach, which suffers from information leakage when LLMs are evaluated on historical data they may have encountered during pretraining. This paper introduces DeepFund, a comprehensive platform for evaluating LLM based trading strategies in a simulated live environment. Our approach implements a multi agent framework where LLMs serve as both analysts and managers, creating a realistic simulation of investment decision making. The platform employs a forward testing methodology that mitigates information leakage by evaluating models on market data released after their training cutoff dates. We provide a web interface that visualizes model performance across different market conditions and investment parameters, enabling detailed comparative analysis. Through DeepFund, we aim to provide a more accurate and fair assessment of LLMs capabilities in fund investment, offering insights into their potential real world applications in financial markets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "DeepFund: Will LLM be Professional at Fund Investment? A Live Arena Perspective" introduces DeepFund, a novel arena platform for evaluating Large Language Models (LLMs) in financial fund investment.  The authors argue that existing benchmarks suffer from data leakage (due to pre-training on historical data), navel-gazing (over-reliance on theory rather than real-time performance), over-intervention (limiting autonomous capabilities through prompt engineering), and maintenance-hard challenges (continuous data and regime shift updates).  DeepFund addresses these issues by simulating a live trading environment using a forward-testing methodology, implementing a multi-agent framework where LLMs act as analysts and managers, and providing a web interface for visualizing model performance across various conditions. The goal is to provide a fairer and more accurate assessment of LLMs' potential in real-world financial applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of a simulated live arena for evaluating LLMs in finance is a significant contribution. The paper directly addresses the issue of data leakage by using forward testing, which is a crucial improvement over traditional backtesting approaches. The multi-agent system design, simulating a more complex investment decision process is also a positive aspect.

*   **Significance:** The work is important because it highlights the limitations of current LLM evaluation benchmarks in the finance domain and offers a practical solution. Successfully addressing data leakage and creating a more realistic evaluation environment could lead to better-informed decisions about LLM adoption in the financial industry.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear identification of critical limitations in existing LLM evaluation methodologies.
    *   A well-defined and comprehensive platform architecture, including a simulated live trading environment and multi-agent framework.
    *   Emphasis on autonomous operation to assess intrinsic LLM capabilities.
    *   Consideration of the maintenance challenges associated with financial benchmarks (data updates, regime shifts).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of Empirical Results:**  The paper mainly outlines the design of the DeepFund platform. It doesn't include substantial empirical results demonstrating the platform's effectiveness in comparison to existing benchmarks. While Figure 3 provides a front-end rendering, it is noted as "in development and does not represent the final effect".  The absence of concrete experimental data significantly weakens the paper's impact.
    *   **Simplifications:** The simulation environment, while designed to be more realistic than backtesting, is still a simplified model of the real-world financial markets. Aspects like transaction costs, market impact, and unforeseen events may not be fully captured.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Agent Complexity:** The paper touches upon the types of analysts (Technical, Fundamental, Insider, Media) but lacks detailed elaboration on the specific methodologies and tools employed by these agents, as well as the LLM prompts utilized. A more extensive explanation would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Impact:** If DeepFund proves to be a robust and accurate evaluation platform, it could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners in the field of financial AI. It has the potential to guide the development of more effective LLM-based investment strategies and accelerate the adoption of AI in the financial industry. The open-source nature of the platform could also foster collaboration and innovation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and important concept, but the lack of experimental validation limits its current impact. The design of the DeepFund platform and its focus on addressing critical limitations in LLM evaluation are promising. However, the absence of empirical results prevents a thorough assessment of the platform's effectiveness. The paper demonstrates awareness of existing gaps in LLM evaluation, and a strong technical architecture; however, without quantitative findings, it is not possible to assess the degree to which DeepFund resolves the limitations discussed.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 6/10

### Knowledge Transfer from LLMs to Provenance Analysis: A Semantic-Augmented Method for APT Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18316v1)
- **Authors**: Fei Zuo, Junghwan Rhee, Yung Ryn Choe
- **Abstract**: Advanced Persistent Threats (APTs) have caused significant losses across a wide range of sectors, including the theft of sensitive data and harm to system integrity. As attack techniques grow increasingly sophisticated and stealthy, the arms race between cyber defenders and attackers continues to intensify. The revolutionary impact of Large Language Models (LLMs) has opened up numerous opportunities in various fields, including cybersecurity. An intriguing question arises: can the extensive knowledge embedded in LLMs be harnessed for provenance analysis and play a positive role in identifying previously unknown malicious events? To seek a deeper understanding of this issue, we propose a new strategy for taking advantage of LLMs in provenance-based threat detection. In our design, the state-of-the-art LLM offers additional details in provenance data interpretation, leveraging their knowledge of system calls, software identity, and high-level understanding of application execution context. The advanced contextualized embedding capability is further utilized to capture the rich semantics of event descriptions. We comprehensively examine the quality of the resulting embeddings, and it turns out that they offer promising avenues. Subsequently, machine learning models built upon these embeddings demonstrated outstanding performance on real-world data. In our evaluation, supervised threat detection achieves a precision of 99.0%, and semi-supervised anomaly detection attains a precision of 96.9%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to Advanced Persistent Threat (APT) detection that leverages the knowledge embedded in Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance provenance analysis.  The core idea is to use LLMs, specifically OpenAI's GPT-4, to augment the semantics of system event descriptions extracted from provenance data.  This is achieved by feeding system event details (e.g., system call name, user, file paths) into GPT-4, which then generates more descriptive explanations. These richer descriptions are then used to generate embeddings, which are subsequently fed into machine learning models (supervised and semi-supervised) for threat detection. The authors demonstrate that this approach improves the accuracy of both supervised and semi-supervised anomaly detection models compared to using traditional embedding techniques like Doc2Vec, particularly when dealing with out-of-vocabulary (OOV) words. They also present a case study demonstrating the approach's ability to detect previously unseen attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the *combination* of LLMs with provenance analysis for APT detection, particularly the emphasis on semantic augmentation of system event descriptions.  While using LLMs for cybersecurity is not entirely new (as the related work section acknowledges), the specific application to provenance data in this way is a valuable contribution. The use of the LLM to both enrich the semantics (via generated text descriptions) *and* to produce the embeddings is a clever and efficient approach. This method overcomes the OOV limitations faced by traditional embedding techniques.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the accuracy and effectiveness of APT detection systems.  APTs are a major threat, and the paper presents convincing evidence that LLMs can help to identify malicious activities that might otherwise be missed. The semi-supervised approach is also practically significant, as it reduces the reliance on labeled data, which can be scarce in real-world security scenarios. The ability to detect previously unseen attacks (demonstrated in the case study) is a particularly important contribution. It suggests that the enriched semantic understanding provided by the LLM enables the system to generalize better.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of APT detection and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The methodology is well-defined and easy to understand, with clear descriptions of the steps involved (pre-processing, explanatory text generation, embedding generation, threat detection).
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a comprehensive evaluation, comparing the proposed approach with baselines and demonstrating its effectiveness on a real-world dataset.
    *   **Case Study:** The inclusion of a case study provides concrete evidence of the approach's ability to detect previously unseen attacks.
    *   **Analysis of LLM Contributions:** The section analyzing the specific contributions of LLM explanations (knowledge of system calls, software identity, execution context, potential suspiciousness) is valuable and insightful.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost of using LLMs. Generating descriptive texts and embeddings using LLMs can be computationally expensive, which might be a limiting factor in real-time deployment. While the use of the 'small' LLM and dimensionality reduction is noted, a more detailed analysis of latency and resource requirements would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Dataset Size and Diversity:** While the ProvSec dataset is a valuable resource, it is relatively small. Evaluating the approach on larger and more diverse datasets would further validate its effectiveness. More discussion on the representativeness of the ProvSec dataset would be beneficial.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Dependency:** The quality of the explanatory texts generated by the LLM depends on the prompts used. The paper mentions prompt engineering but doesn't provide extensive details on how the prompts were designed and optimized. The robustness of the system to variations in prompt design could be a factor.
    *   **Limited Model Exploration:** The paper focuses on a specific set of machine learning models (MLP, GBDT, XGBOD) for threat detection. Exploring other model architectures, such as graph neural networks (GNNs) that can directly leverage the graph structure of provenance data, might yield further improvements.
    *   **Adversarial Attacks on LLM Prompts:** The paper does not consider potential vulnerabilities to adversarial attacks on the LLM's prompts. An attacker might craft inputs designed to mislead the LLM and evade detection. The effect of adversarial inputs needs consideration.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper provides a clear and well-executed demonstration of how LLMs can be used to improve APT detection through provenance analysis. While the core idea (LLMs + provenance) isn't groundbreaking in itself, the *specific approach* of semantic augmentation, the comprehensive evaluation, and the demonstrated ability to detect unseen attacks warrant a high score. The weaknesses related to computational cost, dataset limitations, and prompt engineering dependency are valid concerns that should be addressed in future work. The lack of an adversarial assessment is a significant omission. However, the paper represents a significant step forward in leveraging LLMs for cybersecurity, particularly in the context of provenance analysis.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improved Rates of Differentially Private Nonconvex-Strongly-Concave Minimax Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18317v1)
- **Authors**: Ruijia Zhang, Mingxi Lei, Meng Ding, Zihang Xiang, Jinhui Xu, Di Wang
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we study the problem of (finite sum) minimax optimization in the Differential Privacy (DP) model. Unlike most of the previous studies on the (strongly) convex-concave settings or loss functions satisfying the Polyak-Lojasiewicz condition, here we mainly focus on the nonconvex-strongly-concave one, which encapsulates many models in deep learning such as deep AUC maximization. Specifically, we first analyze a DP version of Stochastic Gradient Descent Ascent (SGDA) and show that it is possible to get a DP estimator whose $l_2$-norm of the gradient for the empirical risk function is upper bounded by $\tilde{O}(\frac{d^{1/4}}{({n\epsilon})^{1/2}})$, where $d$ is the model dimension and $n$ is the sample size. We then propose a new method with less gradient noise variance and improve the upper bound to $\tilde{O}(\frac{d^{1/3}}{(n\epsilon)^{2/3}})$, which matches the best-known result for DP Empirical Risk Minimization with non-convex loss. We also discussed several lower bounds of private minimax optimization. Finally, experiments on AUC maximization, generative adversarial networks, and temporal difference learning with real-world data support our theoretical analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses differentially private (DP) minimax optimization in the non-convex strongly-concave (NC-SC) setting, a scenario common in deep learning.  Unlike previous works that focus on convex-concave or PL-condition loss functions, this paper focuses on NC-SC which is more relevant to deep learning applications.  The paper first analyzes a DP version of Stochastic Gradient Descent Ascent (SGDA) and shows an achievable utility bound.  Then, it proposes a novel method called PrivateDiff Minimax to reduce gradient noise variance and improve the utility bound, matching the best-known results for DP Empirical Risk Minimization with non-convex loss.  Finally, the paper explores the lower bounds for private minimax optimization and demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed method through experiments on AUC maximization, GANs, and temporal difference learning using real-world data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper's primary contribution lies in addressing DP minimax optimization under the NC-SC setting. While DP minimax optimization has been studied, it often restricts the loss function to convex-concave scenarios or functions that satisfy the PL condition. The move to NC-SC, especially for minimax problems, is a welcome step that brings the theory closer to practical deep learning models.  The PrivateDiff Minimax algorithm, which uses gradient differences to reduce noise variance, is novel within the context of DP minimax. The lower bound results also add to the theoretical understanding of the problem. The work of Zhao et al (2023a) also tackles the NC-SC setting, but this work addresses some of its limitations and is also more general.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem. Minimax optimization appears in many important machine learning models. Incorporating privacy guarantees is crucial as these models are often trained on sensitive data. By tackling the NC-SC setting, the research becomes directly relevant to deep learning. The theoretical utility bounds and the matching of the best-known rates for DP-ERM are significant milestones.  The experimental results demonstrate the practical advantages of the proposed method.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Relevant Problem:** NC-SC minimax optimization is a practically important and less explored area in DP.
    * **Novel Algorithm:** PrivateDiff Minimax provides a fresh approach to reducing noise and improving utility.
    * **Theoretical Results:** The paper provides rigorous utility bounds and lower bound results.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results validate the theoretical findings and demonstrate the effectiveness of the method. The paper provides a strong set of experiments.
    * **Addresses Limitations of Existing Work:** The introduction clearly articulates the shortcomings of prior work and positions the contributions effectively.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** While necessary for rigorous analysis, the technical details of the algorithm and proofs may limit accessibility to a broader audience.
    * **Implementation Overhead:**  The PrivateDiff Minimax method introduces some extra computational overhead compared to simpler methods like DP-SGDA. The difference can be a hindrance in problems with computational restrictions.
    * **Limited Lower Bound Analysis:** The lower bound results are a preliminary study. The result is similar to that of the lower bound results in DP-ERM and there is no additional insights about the structure of minimax optimization. More extensive results are beneficial to give a more complete theoretical understanding of the problem.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in DP minimax optimization by providing a more practical algorithm and a theoretical benchmark. The approach of leveraging gradient differences for noise reduction could be adopted and adapted in other DP settings. The focus on NC-SC problems will likely inspire further research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**.  The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a relevant and challenging problem in differentially private machine learning. It presents a novel algorithm with rigorous theoretical guarantees and empirical validation. However, the paper is not without flaws. While the complexity of the mathematical arguments may be necessary, it somewhat limits its broad accessibility. A higher score would be warranted if there is further work on the lower bound analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bridging Writing Manner Gap in Visual Instruction Tuning by Creating LLM-aligned Instructions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18320v1)
- **Authors**: Dong Jing, Nanyi Fei, Zhiwu Lu
- **Abstract**: In the realm of Large Multi-modal Models (LMMs), the instruction quality during the visual instruction tuning stage significantly influences the performance of modality alignment. In this paper, we assess the instruction quality from a unique perspective termed \textbf{Writing Manner}, which encompasses the selection of vocabulary, grammar and sentence structure to convey specific semantics. We argue that there exists a substantial writing manner gap between the visual instructions and the base Large Language Models (LLMs) within LMMs. This gap forces the pre-trained base LLMs to deviate from their original writing styles, leading to capability degradation of both base LLMs and LMMs. To bridge the writing manner gap while preserving the original semantics, we propose directly leveraging the base LLM to align the writing manner of soft-format visual instructions with that of the base LLM itself, resulting in novel LLM-aligned instructions. The manual writing manner evaluation results demonstrate that our approach successfully minimizes the writing manner gap. By utilizing LLM-aligned instructions, the baseline models LLaVA-7B and QwenVL demonstrate enhanced resistance to hallucinations and non-trivial comprehensive improvements across all $15$ visual and language benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "Bridging Writing Manner Gap in Visual Instruction Tuning by Creating LLM-aligned Instructions":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of a "writing manner gap" between visual instruction tuning data and the underlying pre-trained Language Models (LLMs) used within Large Multi-modal Models (LMMs). The authors argue that this gap, encompassing vocabulary, grammar, and sentence structure choices, forces LLMs to deviate from their original writing styles, potentially degrading performance. They propose a method to bridge this gap by leveraging the LLM itself to rewrite and review soft-format visual instructions, aligning their writing manner with the LLM's inherent style while preserving semantics. The approach involves rewriting answers from visual instruction datasets using the LLM, followed by a review stage to ensure quality and maintain semantic consistency. The authors demonstrate through experiments with LLaVA-1.5 and QwenVL that this LLM-aligned instruction tuning improves performance on various visual and language benchmarks and reduces hallucinations.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in identifying and formalizing the "writing manner gap" as a distinct problem in visual instruction tuning for LMMs. While previous works have focused on improving instruction quality through various data augmentation or error correction techniques, this paper offers a unique perspective by focusing on the stylistic alignment between the instruction data and the underlying LLM. The method itself, leveraging the LLM for rewriting and review, is relatively straightforward but its application to this specific problem is new.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is multifaceted. First, it provides a plausible explanation for some of the performance variations observed in LMMs after visual instruction tuning. Second, it offers a simple and effective method to mitigate the identified problem, potentially leading to more robust and reliable LMMs. Third, the improvements on benchmarks like the hallucination benchmark point towards improved faithfulness of instruction following when the data styles are more closely aligned with the pre-trained LLM. The improvement, however, is not extremely dramatic (a few points across different benchmark) and there is no in-depth analysis that the "writing manner gap" is reduced with the presented approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Simple and implementable solution.
    *   Strong empirical results across multiple benchmarks.
    *   Ablation studies help to confirm the effectiveness of the approach (e.g., comparison of models trained with text and instructions data only with those that have visual instructions).
    *   The use of human evaluations regarding stylistic quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "writing manner gap" is somewhat vaguely defined, while the paper provides examples it needs more formal definition (how it's different from regular "style"). A quantitative metric would be much better.
    *   The rewrite/review process relies on the LLM, potentially perpetuating any biases or limitations present in the LLM itself.
    *   While the ablation studies show that the LLM-aligned rewriting contributes to the improvements, it's unclear how much of this improvement is attributed to the actual reduction of the writing manner gap.
    *   The improvements are substantial but not groundbreaking. The performance gains, while consistently positive, are not so transformative as to revolutionize the field.
    *   The human evaluation of the output could be further improved by removing human biases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in visual instruction tuning by highlighting the importance of stylistic alignment. Other researchers will be probably inspired to explore more sophisticated methods for bridging this writing manner gap and might also develop better automatic methods to quantify stylistic similarity between datasets and language models. The approach can be easily integrated with existing data augmentation techniques, further amplifying their benefits.

*   **Conclusion:** Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and addressing a previously overlooked aspect of visual instruction tuning in LMMs. While the method is relatively simple, the empirical results and clear problem definition demonstrate its effectiveness and potential for further development. The paper introduces a new perspective and opens avenue for future research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Plug-and-Play Interpretable Responsible Text-to-Image Generation via Dual-Space Multi-facet Concept Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18324v1)
- **Authors**: Basim Azam, Naveed Akhtar
- **Abstract**: Ethical issues around text-to-image (T2I) models demand a comprehensive control over the generative content. Existing techniques addressing these issues for responsible T2I models aim for the generated content to be fair and safe (non-violent/explicit). However, these methods remain bounded to handling the facets of responsibility concepts individually, while also lacking in interpretability. Moreover, they often require alteration to the original model, which compromises the model performance. In this work, we propose a unique technique to enable responsible T2I generation by simultaneously accounting for an extensive range of concepts for fair and safe content generation in a scalable manner. The key idea is to distill the target T2I pipeline with an external plug-and-play mechanism that learns an interpretable composite responsible space for the desired concepts, conditioned on the target T2I pipeline. We use knowledge distillation and concept whitening to enable this. At inference, the learned space is utilized to modulate the generative content. A typical T2I pipeline presents two plug-in points for our approach, namely; the text embedding space and the diffusion model latent space. We develop modules for both points and show the effectiveness of our approach with a range of strong results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper proposes a plug-and-play method to improve the responsible generation of images using Text-to-Image (T2I) models.  The key idea is to distill knowledge from a pre-trained T2I model (like Stable Diffusion) into an external module that learns an interpretable, composite space for concepts related to fairness and safety (e.g., race, gender, age, safety). This external module is conditioned on the original T2I model and uses knowledge distillation and concept whitening to enable more precise control. The approach can be applied at two points within the T2I pipeline: the text embedding space and the latent diffusion space, resulting in two add-on modules: RICE (Responsible Interpretable CLIP Embedding) and RIIDL (Responsible Interpretable Intermediate Diffusion Latents).  The distilled modules are then used to modulate the original T2I model's output, promoting fairer and safer image generation. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of the method with experiments showing improved fairness metrics and reduced generation of inappropriate content.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its dual-space approach combined with a plug-and-play architecture for responsible T2I generation. While individual components like knowledge distillation, concept whitening, and latent space manipulation have been explored before, their combination in this specific context and the ability to operate on both the text embedding and diffusion latent space concurrently is relatively novel. It offers a more comprehensive control mechanism compared to previous methods that often focus on a single aspect or require fine-tuning of the original model. The focus on interpretable control is also a positive aspect.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of generative AI: the potential for biased and unsafe content generation by T2I models. Developing techniques that promote fairness and safety is essential for responsible deployment of these powerful models. The plug-and-play nature of the approach is significant because it avoids the need to retrain or modify the original T2I model, which is a major advantage in terms of practicality and maintaining performance. Also, it makes it easy to extend with other attributes.
The work also has potential impact as a tool for understanding biases within text embeddings and diffusion models and opens up avenues for further research into disentangled representations in generative models.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Dual-space control:** Operating on both text embedding and latent space provides finer-grained control and addresses different aspects of the bias problem.
    * **Plug-and-play:** Maintains the original model's performance and reduces the need for retraining.
    * **Interpretability:**  Striving for interpretable control is crucial for debugging and understanding the impact of interventions.
    * **Scalability:** The modular design and concept whitening facilitates scaling the method to a wide range of responsible concepts.
    * **Empirical Evaluation:** Experiments demonstrate improved fairness metrics and reduced unsafe content generation across several aspects.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Hyperparameter Tuning:**  The approach introduces multiple hyperparameters (e.g.,  α, λ's) that require tuning. While the paper claims that these parameters are easy to interpret, finding optimal values can still be challenging and potentially dataset/model dependent.
    * **Definition of 'Responsibility':** The definition of "responsible" generation relies on a pre-defined set of attributes (Aresp). While extendable, the inherent limitations of defining all concepts related to fairness, safety, and ethics remain.
    * **Potential for Misuse:** The authors acknowledge the potential for misuse by replacing Aresp with concepts that promote unsafe/unfair generation. While this is an inherent risk with many AI tools, a deeper discussion of mitigation strategies would be beneficial.
    * **Dependence on Teacher Model:** Performance depends on teacher T2I model.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective technique for responsible T2I generation.  It could encourage further research into plug-and-play methods, dual-space control, and interpretable representations in generative models. It can potentially influence how the existing techniques will deal with ethical issues.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**  The paper demonstrates a reasonably innovative approach to a significant problem.  The dual-space plug-and-play architecture is a substantial improvement over many existing methods and offers a good balance between control, performance, and interpretability.  However, the dependence on hyperparameter tuning, the inherent challenges in defining "responsibility", and potential for misuse are limitations that prevent a higher score. Furthermore, the score is not higher because of reliance on the teacher T2I performance.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Optimizing Influence Campaigns: Nudging under Bounded Confidence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18331v1)
- **Authors**: Yen-Shao Chen, Tauhid Zaman
- **Abstract**: Influence campaigns in online social networks are often run by organizations, political parties, and nation states to influence large audiences. These campaigns are employed through the use of agents in the network that share persuasive content. Yet, their impact might be minimal if the audiences remain unswayed, often due to the bounded confidence phenomenon, where only a narrow spectrum of viewpoints can influence them. Here we show that to persuade under bounded confidence, an agent must nudge its targets to gradually shift their opinions. Using a control theory approach, we show how to construct an agent's nudging policy under the bounded confidence opinion dynamics model and also how to select targets for multiple agents in an influence campaign on a social network. Simulations on real Twitter networks show that a multi-agent nudging policy can shift the mean opinion, decrease opinion polarization, or even increase it. We find that our nudging based policies outperform other common techniques that do not consider the bounded confidence effect. Finally, we show how to craft prompts for large language models, such as ChatGPT, to generate text-based content for real nudging policies. This illustrates the practical feasibility of our approach, allowing one to go from mathematical nudging policies to real social media content.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of optimizing influence campaigns on social networks, specifically considering the bounded confidence phenomenon, where individuals are less susceptible to opinions far removed from their own. The authors propose a control theory approach to design nudging policies, where agents gradually shift targets' opinions over time. They model opinion dynamics using differential equations and develop algorithms for both target selection (using a greedy approach) and dynamic content creation (solving for time-varying content opinions using control theory principles). The approach is evaluated through simulations on real Twitter networks, comparing it against a DeGroot model-based approach. The paper also explores how to translate numerical opinion policies into actual social media content using large language models (LLMs) like ChatGPT.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integration of several elements: 1) it combines the bounded confidence model, which is a more realistic representation of opinion dynamics, with control theory techniques; 2) it develops algorithms specifically tailored for influence campaigns under the bounded confidence model, considering both target selection and dynamic content creation; and 3) demonstrates a practical pathway to implement the policies using LLMs.

However, some elements are less novel: the greedy approach for target selection is well-established (though modified here for the bounded confidence setting), and the use of LLMs for content generation, while practical, builds upon existing research in that area. The control theory is also an established technique. The specific combination and the adaptation to the bounded confidence setting with practical implementation via LLMs are the primary drivers of novelty.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the potential to create more effective and nuanced influence campaigns. By acknowledging bounded confidence and using a nudging approach, the authors address a key limitation of traditional influence strategies that assume universal persuasibility. The experimental findings highlighting the out-performance of nudging policies compared to static (DeGroot) policies underline the impact. The integration of LLMs for content generation offers a pragmatic solution for real-world implementation, making the proposed framework potentially more useful to campaign managers and similar practitioners.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a relevant and important problem with practical applications in online influence.
*   **Realistic Model:** Incorporating the bounded confidence model is a crucial improvement over simpler linear models.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The control theory approach is well-defined, and the algorithms are clearly presented.
*   **Empirical Validation:** Simulations on real-world Twitter networks lend credibility to the findings.
*   **Practical Implementation:** The use of LLMs to translate opinion policies into content is a significant step towards practical implementation.
*   The paper provides theoretical analysis with explicit theorems and proposition, even though some of them prove negative results (like non-submodularity), the fact that they are explicitly formalized provides rigor.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Greedy Approach Limitations:** The greedy target selection, while scalable, comes with known sub-optimality.  The paper itself notes that under the greedy approach, adding agents can sometimes *decrease* objective value, thus, limiting it.  The lack of performance guarantees is a significant weakness.
*   **Model Simplifications:** The opinion dynamics model, while an improvement over linear models, is still a simplification of real-world social interactions.
*   **LLM Dependence:** The implementation relies on the capabilities and behavior of LLMs, which can be unpredictable and subject to change.  The paper doesn't deeply examine the ethical implications of using LLMs for this purpose.
*   The theorems, while rigorous, aren't particularly surprising and the paper doesn't make significant contribution to theory. The major contributions are in system implementation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field of influence campaigns by incorporating a realistic model of opinion dynamics and providing a practical roadmap for implementation. However, the reliance on a greedy heuristic, the model simplifications, and dependence on LLMs prevent it from achieving a higher score. Also, the theoretical contributions and proofs while rigorous, are not ground-breaking and mostly establish negative results.

### Coeff-Tuning: A Graph Filter Subspace View for Tuning Attention-Based Large Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18337v1)
- **Authors**: Zichen Miao, Wei Chen, Qiang Qiu
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based large pre-trained models have shown remarkable generalization ability, and various parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods have been proposed to customize these models on downstream tasks with minimal computational and memory budgets. Previous PEFT methods are primarily designed from a tensor-decomposition perspective that tries to effectively tune the linear transformation by finding the smallest subset of parameters to train. Our study adopts an orthogonal view by representing the attention operation as a graph convolution and formulating the multi-head attention maps as a convolutional filter subspace, with each attention map as a subspace element. In this paper, we propose to tune the large pre-trained transformers by learning a small set of combination coefficients that construct a more expressive filter subspace from the original multi-head attention maps. We show analytically and experimentally that the tuned filter subspace can effectively expand the feature space of the multi-head attention and further enhance the capacity of transformers. We further stabilize the fine-tuning with a residual parameterization of the tunable subspace coefficients, and enhance the generalization with a regularization design by directly applying dropout on the tunable coefficient during training. The tunable coefficients take a tiny number of parameters and can be combined with previous PEFT methods in a plug-and-play manner. Extensive experiments show that our approach achieves superior performances than PEFT baselines with neglectable additional parameters.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Coeff-Tuning," a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) method for large, attention-based models.  The key idea is to view the multi-head attention mechanism as a graph convolution operation with a filter subspace.  Coeff-Tuning proposes learning a small set of combination coefficients to construct a more expressive filter subspace from the original multi-head attention maps.  The method includes a residual parameterization to stabilize fine-tuning and regularization via dropout on the tunable coefficients. Experiments demonstrate superior performance compared to PEFT baselines across various tasks and transformer architectures with a negligible increase in parameters. The method is designed to be plug-and-play with existing PEFT methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel perspective on attention mechanisms by framing them as graph convolutions and filter subspaces.  The idea of tuning the filter subspace by learning combination coefficients is also new.  While PEFT methods are already a well-explored space, the geometric interpretation and subspace tuning approach offer a fresh angle. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental. PEFT methods are rapidly evolving, and this specific approach builds upon existing techniques. It is a twist, not a completely revolutionary idea.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the improved performance and parameter efficiency.  Achieving better results with minimal additional parameters is crucial for deploying large models on resource-constrained devices and enabling faster experimentation. The plug-and-play nature allows for easier adoption within existing PEFT pipelines.  The method has a practical contribution, but the magnitude of the improvement relative to the complexity of the idea needs careful consideration. The tasks chosen are standard benchmarks, which aids comparability, but also slightly reduces the overall perceived impact. Demonstrating efficacy on a complex, previously unsolved problem could have substantially increased the perceived significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-written presentation. The paper clearly explains the method and its motivation.
    *   Strong theoretical justification: providing analytical insights into the effectiveness of the coefficient tuning approach.
    *   Comprehensive experimental validation across various tasks and architectures.
    *   Demonstrated improvement over strong PEFT baselines.
    *   Practicality - easy integration with other PEFT techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance improvements, while consistent, might be marginal in some cases. It's important to carefully evaluate the effect sizes. Are the gains substantial or only statistically significant?
    *   While the method is described as "plug-and-play," the implementation details and potential interactions with different PEFT techniques could be complex, depending on the implementation. This requires a more thorough investigation and guidance.
    *   The ablation studies provide some insight, but further analysis into the sensitivity of the performance to specific hyperparameters and architectural choices could be beneficial.
    *   The method's dependence on the specific attention layer formulation might limit its applicability to newer transformer architectures with different attention mechanisms.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in PEFT by promoting the idea of geometric interpretations of attention mechanisms and subspace tuning. It provides a promising direction for exploring novel ways to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of fine-tuning large models.
**Rigorous Rationale:**

The core idea of viewing attention through the lens of graph convolution and filter subspaces is interesting, but its novelty is somewhat mitigated by the existing literature on PEFT techniques and geometric interpretations of neural networks. It introduces a specific, technically sound method within a well-defined research area. The significance is mainly due to its empirical effectiveness – demonstrating improved performance with minimal added parameters. The extensive experiments lend credibility to the findings, but the gains, in some cases, appear marginal. The paper's strength lies in its clarity, well-designed experiments, and practical integration. However, the novelty is more incremental than groundbreaking. The weaknesses also reflect the need for a deeper and more systematic analysis and comparison.

### Latent Embedding Adaptation for Human Preference Alignment in Diffusion Planners
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18347v1)
- **Authors**: Wen Zheng Terence Ng, Jianda Chen, Yuan Xu, Tianwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: This work addresses the challenge of personalizing trajectories generated in automated decision-making systems by introducing a resource-efficient approach that enables rapid adaptation to individual users' preferences. Our method leverages a pretrained conditional diffusion model with Preference Latent Embeddings (PLE), trained on a large, reward-free offline dataset. The PLE serves as a compact representation for capturing specific user preferences. By adapting the pretrained model using our proposed preference inversion method, which directly optimizes the learnable PLE, we achieve superior alignment with human preferences compared to existing solutions like Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) and Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA). To better reflect practical applications, we create a benchmark experiment using real human preferences on diverse, high-reward trajectories.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a new method called "Latent Embedding Adaptation for Human Preference Alignment in Diffusion Planners" to personalize trajectories generated by automated decision-making systems.  It uses a pre-trained conditional diffusion model and introduces Preference Latent Embeddings (PLEs) to capture individual user preferences.  The core idea is to adapt the pre-trained model using a "preference inversion" method, which directly optimizes the learnable PLEs based on a small amount of user-specific preference data. The authors demonstrate that their approach achieves superior alignment with human preferences compared to Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) and Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA).  They also create a benchmark experiment using real human preferences on diverse, high-reward trajectories to evaluate their method in a practical setting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques and its application to trajectory personalization.  Diffusion models have been used for planning, and preference learning techniques like RLHF and DPO are established. The introduction of PLEs is a reasonable approach to represent user preferences compactly.  The "preference inversion" technique, borrowing from image manipulation techniques for diffusion models, is a relatively novel adaptation strategy.  However, the individual components are not entirely groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem – efficiently personalizing automated systems to individual user preferences.  The practical benefits of such a system are clear, especially in applications like autonomous driving and robotics. The resource-efficient adaptation approach, allowing for updates with minimal data and computational overhead, is particularly valuable. The real-world experiment with human preference data adds practical relevance to the research. The experiments show the method outperforming RLHF and LoRA, which are significant findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method is computationally efficient, which is crucial for real-world applications.
    *   It achieves superior alignment with human preferences compared to existing methods.
    *   The benchmark experiment uses real human preferences, making the results more relevant.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The components themselves are not entirely novel, but rather the combination of existing techniques.
    *   The dependency on a pre-trained diffusion model might be a limiting factor. The performance hinges on the quality of pretraining.
    *   While real human preferences are used, the user study involves only three users, which limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   The method is limited to trajectory personalization and may not be directly applicable to other domains.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    The paper has the potential to influence the development of more personalized and adaptive automated systems. The resource-efficient adaptation method could enable wider deployment in resource-constrained environments. Further research might explore the applicability of PLEs to other domains and the integration of this approach with other preference learning techniques.

**Score:** 7.  While the core components are not entirely novel, the combination of pre-trained diffusion model, PLEs, and preference inversion, along with the convincing experimental results and practical relevance, make it a solid contribution. The use of real human preferences increases the significance, but the limited number of users restricts the impact. The method also appears tailored specifically to trajectory personalization. A score of 7 reflects a significant and novel combination of methods that provides a practical advancement in the field.

### Diffusion-4K: Ultra-High-Resolution Image Synthesis with Latent Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18352v1)
- **Authors**: Jinjin Zhang, Qiuyu Huang, Junjie Liu, Xiefan Guo, Di Huang
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we present Diffusion-4K, a novel framework for direct ultra-high-resolution image synthesis using text-to-image diffusion models. The core advancements include: (1) Aesthetic-4K Benchmark: addressing the absence of a publicly available 4K image synthesis dataset, we construct Aesthetic-4K, a comprehensive benchmark for ultra-high-resolution image generation. We curated a high-quality 4K dataset with carefully selected images and captions generated by GPT-4o. Additionally, we introduce GLCM Score and Compression Ratio metrics to evaluate fine details, combined with holistic measures such as FID, Aesthetics and CLIPScore for a comprehensive assessment of ultra-high-resolution images. (2) Wavelet-based Fine-tuning: we propose a wavelet-based fine-tuning approach for direct training with photorealistic 4K images, applicable to various latent diffusion models, demonstrating its effectiveness in synthesizing highly detailed 4K images. Consequently, Diffusion-4K achieves impressive performance in high-quality image synthesis and text prompt adherence, especially when powered by modern large-scale diffusion models (e.g., SD3-2B and Flux-12B). Extensive experimental results from our benchmark demonstrate the superiority of Diffusion-4K in ultra-high-resolution image synthesis.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a rigorous evaluation of the Diffusion-4K paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Diffusion-4K: Ultra-High-Resolution Image Synthesis with Latent Diffusion Models" introduces a framework called Diffusion-4K designed for generating ultra-high-resolution (4K) images directly using text-to-image diffusion models. The core contributions include:

1.  **Aesthetic-4K Benchmark:** A new 4K image dataset with high-quality images and GPT-4o generated captions is introduced to address the lack of suitable datasets for 4K image synthesis. They also propose GLCM Score and Compression Ratio metrics to evaluate fine details, in addition to common metrics such as FID and CLIPScore.
2.  **Wavelet-based Fine-tuning:** A novel fine-tuning approach is proposed that leverages wavelet transforms to enhance high-frequency details in the generated images while preserving overall structure. This method is designed to be compatible with various latent diffusion models.
The authors demonstrate through experiments that Diffusion-4K, especially when combined with large-scale diffusion models like SD3 and Flux, achieves impressive results in 4K image synthesis, exhibiting both high quality and adherence to text prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Aesthetic-4K Dataset:** The creation of a dedicated, high-quality 4K image dataset with meticulously crafted captions is a significant contribution. The lack of such datasets has been a bottleneck in the field, and the release of this benchmark will undoubtedly facilitate further research. Including metrics such as GLCM score and compression ratio for 4K images is also novel.
*   **Wavelet-based Fine-tuning:** While wavelet transforms are not entirely new in image processing, their specific application to fine-tuning latent diffusion models for enhanced detail in *ultra-high-resolution* image synthesis is relatively novel. The core idea of focusing on high-frequency details during fine-tuning is intuitively sound and has been demonstrably effective.
* The authors also use the LLM GPT-40 for their captioning work, which helps to make the generated images adhere to the text prompts provided.

**Significance:**

*   **Advancement of the Field:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the field of text-to-image synthesis by enabling the direct generation of 4K images. This has significant implications for various applications, including content creation, entertainment, and scientific visualization.
*   **Practical Impact:** The proposed framework is designed to be compatible with existing latent diffusion models, making it readily adoptable by researchers and practitioners. The code will also be released to help further adoption of their method.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The rigorous evaluation, incorporating both standard metrics and the newly introduced metrics, provides a comprehensive assessment of the performance of Diffusion-4K. The human-centric evaluation adds further credibility to the findings.

**Strengths:**

*   Well-motivated problem and clear contributions.
*   Sound technical approach with a focus on practical applications.
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation using both quantitative and qualitative measures.
*   Introduction of a valuable new dataset and metrics for 4K image synthesis.
*   Clear writing and presentation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The Wavelet-based Fine-tuning component, while effective, might be somewhat incremental rather than a radical breakthrough in diffusion models.
*   The GLCM score and compression ratio metrics seem somewhat basic and could potentially be supplemented by more sophisticated detail-focused metrics in future work. The human evaluation seems a little too small in scale (only 5 human evaluators).
*   The experiments are conducted primarily on two models (SD3 and Flux), so, further tests with other models and datasets can help to prove the robustness of the methods proposed in the paper.

**Potential Influence:**

*   The Aesthetic-4K dataset is likely to become a standard benchmark for 4K image synthesis research.
*   The Wavelet-based Fine-tuning approach could inspire further research on detail enhancement techniques for diffusion models.
*   The paper will likely spur further exploration of direct 4K image synthesis using latent diffusion models.

**Overall, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field of text-to-image synthesis, addressing a crucial gap in high-resolution image generation. The introduction of the Aesthetic-4K dataset and the Wavelet-based Fine-tuning approach are notable advances that will likely influence future research.**

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid advancement in the field with practical value, a new dataset, and good empirical results. While the core ideas are not revolutionary, the combination of the dataset, fine-tuning method, and rigorous evaluation warrants a high score. The weaknesses are relatively minor and don't significantly detract from the overall contribution. The high utility of the new dataset and solid execution of methods leads to the score of 7.5.

### J&H: Evaluating the Robustness of Large Language Models Under Knowledge-Injection Attacks in Legal Domain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18360v1)
- **Authors**: Yiran Hu, Huanghai Liu, Qingjing Chen, Ning Zheng, Chong Wang, Yun Liu, Charles L. A. Clarke, Weixing Shen
- **Abstract**: As the scale and capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) increase, their applications in knowledge-intensive fields such as legal domain have garnered widespread attention. However, it remains doubtful whether these LLMs make judgments based on domain knowledge for reasoning. If LLMs base their judgments solely on specific words or patterns, rather than on the underlying logic of the language, the ''LLM-as-judges'' paradigm poses substantial risks in the real-world applications. To address this question, we propose a method of legal knowledge injection attacks for robustness testing, thereby inferring whether LLMs have learned legal knowledge and reasoning logic. In this paper, we propose J&H: an evaluation framework for detecting the robustness of LLMs under knowledge injection attacks in the legal domain. The aim of the framework is to explore whether LLMs perform deductive reasoning when accomplishing legal tasks. To further this aim, we have attacked each part of the reasoning logic underlying these tasks (major premise, minor premise, and conclusion generation). We have collected mistakes that legal experts might make in judicial decisions in the real world, such as typos, legal synonyms, inaccurate external legal statutes retrieval. However, in real legal practice, legal experts tend to overlook these mistakes and make judgments based on logic. However, when faced with these errors, LLMs are likely to be misled by typographical errors and may not utilize logic in their judgments. We conducted knowledge injection attacks on existing general and domain-specific LLMs. Current LLMs are not robust against the attacks employed in our experiments. In addition we propose and compare several methods to enhance the knowledge robustness of LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "J&H: Evaluating the Robustness of Large Language Models Under Knowledge-Injection Attacks in Legal Domain" introduces a framework, J&H, for evaluating the robustness of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the legal domain. The framework uses knowledge injection attacks based on the syllogistic reasoning process (major premise, minor premise, conclusion) to test whether LLMs rely on domain knowledge and logical reasoning, or merely on surface-level patterns.  The authors inject errors at each stage of the syllogism, such as incorrect legal articles, synonymous substitutions, and irrelevant information. The performance of existing general and legal-specific LLMs is evaluated using the framework.  The paper finds that current LLMs are not robust to these knowledge injection attacks and proposes three methods – RAG, Chain of Thought (COT), and few-shot learning – to enhance robustness, albeit with limited success. The authors conclude that improvements should be targeted at the pre-training and fine-tuning stages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in the targeted approach to testing LLM robustness *specifically* within the legal domain and in the use of a syllogistic reasoning framework to structure the attacks. While adversarial attacks on LLMs are not new, this paper refines the attacks to focus on knowledge and logical reasoning, rather than just semantic or syntactic manipulations. The annotation of legal concepts like similar crimes and legal synonyms for creating attacks is another valuable contribution. However, using a syllogism is somewhat simplified, and the level of logical reasoning might be deeper in real-world legal scenarios.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a critical concern regarding the reliability of LLMs in high-stakes domains like law. If LLMs are to be used as domain experts, it's crucial to ensure that they can reason correctly and are not easily misled by errors or irrelevant information.  The framework provides a systematic way to assess this.  The findings highlighting the vulnerability of current LLMs are important, as they expose the risks of deploying these models without proper validation. The investigation of mitigation strategies, even with limited success, points to future research directions.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of trustworthiness and robustness of LLMs in the legal domain.
*   **Structured Framework:** The J&H framework provides a structured methodology for evaluating LLM robustness.
*   **Domain-Specific Attacks:**  The attacks are tailored to the legal domain, using legal synonyms and legal concept confusions, making them more relevant than generic adversarial attacks.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:**  The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments with various LLMs.
*   **Open-Source Code:** Providing the code increases the reproducibility and accessibility of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simplified Reasoning:** The syllogistic reasoning framework, while useful, is a simplified representation of legal reasoning. Real legal arguments are often more complex and involve nuances not captured by this framework.
*   **Limited Mitigation Success:** The proposed mitigation strategies (RAG, COT, Few-shot) did not yield substantial improvements.  This suggests that prompt engineering alone might not be sufficient to address the underlying problem, but the failure needs more rigorous analysis about why the techniques failed.
*   **Limited Scope of Legal Domain:**  The scope of the legal tasks evaluated may be somewhat narrow. Further expansion to other common legal tasks could make the results more convincing.
*   **Dataset Size and Complexity:** While the number of cases is reasonable, the complexity of individual legal cases might vary significantly. Ensuring a representative distribution of case complexity would enhance the study's validity.
*   **Black-Box Analysis:** The analysis lacks an in-depth investigation of *why* the LLMs fail in specific instances. A more detailed analysis of the LLMs' internal representations or attention mechanisms might provide insights into their vulnerabilities.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by highlighting the need for robust and reliable LLMs in the legal domain. The J&H framework provides a systematic approach to evaluating the robustness of LLMs and provides insight on where current LLMs struggle to perform. However, the limited success of the mitigation strategies, and the lack of exploration into the limitations of why the models failed weaken the paper's impact. The simplification of the logical reasoning involved in legal tasks and the lack of in-depth analysis of the failure cases also prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### DiffusedWrinkles: A Diffusion-Based Model for Data-Driven Garment Animation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18370v1)
- **Authors**: Raquel Vidaurre, Elena Garces, Dan Casas
- **Abstract**: We present a data-driven method for learning to generate animations of 3D garments using a 2D image diffusion model. In contrast to existing methods, typically based on fully connected networks, graph neural networks, or generative adversarial networks, which have difficulties to cope with parametric garments with fine wrinkle detail, our approach is able to synthesize high-quality 3D animations for a wide variety of garments and body shapes, while being agnostic to the garment mesh topology. Our key idea is to represent 3D garment deformations as a 2D layout-consistent texture that encodes 3D offsets with respect to a parametric garment template. Using this representation, we encode a large dataset of garments simulated in various motions and shapes and train a novel conditional diffusion model that is able to synthesize high-quality pose-shape-and-design dependent 3D garment deformations. Since our model is generative, we can synthesize various plausible deformations for a given target pose, shape, and design. Additionally, we show that we can further condition our model using an existing garment state, which enables the generation of temporally coherent sequences.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DiffusedWrinkles: A Diffusion-Based Model for Data-Driven Garment Animation":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel data-driven approach to generate animations of 3D garments using a 2D image diffusion model. The key idea is to represent 3D garment deformations as a 2D layout-consistent texture encoding 3D offsets from a garment template. The method involves training a conditional diffusion model on a large dataset of simulated garments in various poses and shapes. This model synthesizes high-quality pose, shape, and design-dependent 3D garment deformations. The approach is topology-agnostic and capable of generating fine-scale wrinkles. Furthermore, the model can be conditioned on existing garment states to produce temporally coherent animation sequences. The results demonstrate the ability to generate realistic 3D garment animations for a range of garments, body shapes, and motions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Representation:** The 2D texture-based representation of 3D garment deformations is a clever way to leverage the power of image diffusion models while remaining agnostic to garment mesh topology. This is a significant advantage over methods that directly manipulate 3D vertices.
    *   **High-Quality Results:** The paper demonstrates the ability to generate visually plausible and detailed garment animations, including fine wrinkles, which is a challenging aspect for many existing learning-based methods.
    *   **Temporal Coherence:** The conditioning on previous garment states to create temporally coherent animations addresses a critical issue in garment animation, leading to more realistic and stable sequences.
    *   **Versatility:** The model handles variations in pose, body shape, and garment design, showcasing its adaptability and potential for broader application.
    *   **Clear Improvements:** The paper provides clear evidence (both quantitative and qualitative) that the temporal coherent model improves upon non-temporal diffusion models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Data Dependency:** The method relies on a large, simulated dataset, which might limit its ability to generalize to real-world garments or unseen materials with different physical properties. While the paper claims the model generalizes to unseen combinations of garment parameters, this is still within the confines of the simulation parameters.
    *   **Collision Handling:** As acknowledged by the authors, body-garment collisions are still a challenge. The current approach of pushing problematic vertices outward is a rudimentary solution that might not always produce realistic results.
    *   **Limited Dynamics:** The model does not explicitly simulate garment dynamics, which can impact the realism of the animations. The temporally-coherent diffusion helps mitigate this, but it's not a substitute for actual physics simulation.
    *   **Lack of fine-grained Control**: The proposed method produces realistic but ultimately "black box" results. Fine-grained editing of the garment behavior is difficult. The presented method still lacks the directness of traditional physics based simulation.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The use of a 2D image diffusion model for 3D garment animation is a novel application of this technology, and the specific way the UV-texture is used adds to that novelty.
    *   The topology-agnostic representation and the ability to generate fine-scale wrinkles are significant improvements over existing methods.
    *   The temporal coherence mechanism addresses a practical limitation in garment animation, enhancing the realism of the generated sequences.
    *   The work shows the potential of diffusion models in garment animation by providing a generative approach.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could inspire further research in using image-based diffusion models for other 3D animation tasks.
    *   The 2D texture-based representation could be adopted in other garment simulation or animation pipelines.
    *   The temporal coherence mechanism could be extended to other animation domains.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and promising approach, several limitations prevent it from receiving a higher score. The reliance on simulated data, the basic collision handling, and the lack of explicit dynamics all impact the overall realism and applicability of the method. However, the novel representation, high-quality results, and the temporal coherence mechanism are significant contributions that deserve recognition. The paper effectively adapts an existing technique for a novel usage in 3D garment animation, with clear demonstration of improvements. The paper addresses a significant challenge in Computer Graphics - creating realistic and efficient garment animations.

### Maximum Redundancy Pruning: A Principle-Driven Layerwise Sparsity Allocation for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18377v1)
- **Authors**: Chang Gao, Kang Zhao, Jianfei Chen, Liping Jing
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities, but their enormous size poses significant challenges for deployment in real-world applications. To address this issue, researchers have sought to apply network pruning techniques to LLMs. A critical challenge in pruning is allocation the sparsity for each layer. Recent sparsity allocation methods is often based on heuristics or search that can easily lead to suboptimal performance. In this paper, we conducted an extensive investigation into various LLMs and revealed three significant discoveries: (1) the layerwise pruning sensitivity (LPS) of LLMs is highly non-uniform, (2) the choice of pruning metric affects LPS, and (3) the performance of a sparse model is related to the uniformity of its layerwise redundancy level. Based on these observations, we propose that the layerwise sparsity of LLMs should adhere to three principles: \emph{non-uniformity}, \emph{pruning metric dependency}, and \emph{uniform layerwise redundancy level} in the pruned model.
