To this end, we proposed Maximum Redundancy Pruning (MRP), an iterative pruning algorithm that prunes in the most redundant layers (\emph{i.e.}, those with the highest non-outlier ratio) at each iteration. The achieved layerwise sparsity aligns with the outlined principles. We conducted extensive experiments on publicly available LLMs, including the LLaMA2 and OPT, across various benchmarks. Experimental results validate the effectiveness of MRP, demonstrating its superiority over previous methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Maximum Redundancy Pruning: A Principle-Driven Layerwise Sparsity Allocation for LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of layerwise sparsity allocation in LLM pruning. Through extensive empirical analysis of various LLMs (LLaMA2, OPT, etc.), the authors identify three key principles: 1) non-uniform layer sensitivity to pruning, 2) pruning metric dependency of layer sensitivity, and 3) the correlation between sparse model performance and the uniformity of layerwise redundancy levels.  Based on these principles, they propose a novel iterative pruning algorithm called Maximum Redundancy Pruning (MRP).  MRP quantifies the redundancy of each layer using a non-outlier ratio and iteratively prunes the most redundant layers until the target sparsity is achieved.  Experimental results demonstrate that MRP outperforms existing methods in language modeling and zero-shot classification tasks.  Notably, they show that a pruned LLaMA2-13B model (using MRP and LoRA) can even slightly outperform a LLaMA2-7B model trained from scratch.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's main novelty lies in the systematic empirical investigation leading to the identification of the three key principles guiding layerwise sparsity allocation. While layerwise pruning and the concept of differing layer sensitivity have been explored before, the explicit formulation of these principles, especially the "uniform layerwise redundancy" principle, is a valuable contribution.  The MRP algorithm itself, while iterative and based on the non-outlier ratio, is a relatively straightforward implementation of these principles.  The "sensitivity reversal" finding with respect to pruning metrics is also a nice observation. However, the individual components (iterative pruning, redundancy estimation) are not entirely new in isolation.

*   **Significance:** The paper has significant practical implications.  The demonstrated performance improvements of MRP over existing methods on popular LLMs suggest that it could be a valuable tool for reducing the size and computational cost of these models without significant performance degradation.  The fact that a pruned, fine-tuned 13B model can outperform a 7B model trained from scratch is a compelling result that highlights the potential benefits of this approach. The three principles provide a guiding framework that could influence future research on LLM pruning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Analysis:**  The paper is grounded in a thorough empirical study involving multiple LLMs, pruning metrics, and tasks.  This provides strong evidence to support the identified principles.
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of layerwise sparsity allocation and the limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Algorithm:** The MRP algorithm is clearly described and relatively easy to implement.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the effectiveness of MRP.
    *   **Practical Implications:**  The demonstrated performance gains and potential for model size reduction have significant practical implications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the empirical evidence is strong, the paper lacks a deeper theoretical justification for why the identified principles are optimal.  Why should layerwise redundancy be uniform?  What theoretical reasons might explain the sensitivity reversal phenomenon?
    *   **MRP Algorithm Simplicity:**  The MRP algorithm is relatively simple.  While effective, it could potentially be improved by incorporating more sophisticated optimization techniques or adaptive redundancy estimation methods. The gains relative to other fairly heuristic methods like OWL are respectable, but not *massive*.
    *   **Dependency on Dataset (C4) for Redundancy Estimation:**  The non-outlier ratio is calculated based on the C4 dataset. The choice of this dataset might influence the resulting sparsity allocation, and it's unclear how sensitive the results are to this choice. The paper would be strengthened by showing this wasn't the case.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on LLM pruning by providing a clear set of principles to guide layerwise sparsity allocation. It could also inspire the development of more sophisticated pruning algorithms based on these principles. The open-sourcing of the code would also increase impact.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7** to this paper.  It's a solid contribution with well-executed empirical analysis, a clear problem formulation, and promising experimental results. However, the lack of deeper theoretical justification and the relative simplicity of the MRP algorithm prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the iterative approach is logical and efficient, the lack of more adaptive methods limits the "novelty" and "significance" of the solution relative to the problem being tackled. The paper fills an important gap by providing insights on how to determine the importance of each layer but more can be done to strengthen it, and is not quite "groundbreaking."

Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Resource-Efficient Motion Control for Video Generation via Dynamic Mask Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18386v1)
- **Authors**: Sicong Feng, Jielong Yang, Li Peng
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models bring new vitality to visual content creation. However, current text-to-video generation models still face significant challenges such as high training costs, substantial data requirements, and difficulties in maintaining consistency between given text and motion of the foreground object. To address these challenges, we propose mask-guided video generation, which can control video generation through mask motion sequences, while requiring limited training data. Our model enhances existing architectures by incorporating foreground masks for precise text-position matching and motion trajectory control. Through mask motion sequences, we guide the video generation process to maintain consistent foreground objects throughout the sequence. Additionally, through a first-frame sharing strategy and autoregressive extension approach, we achieve more stable and longer video generation. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that this approach excels in various video generation tasks, such as video editing and generating artistic videos, outperforming previous methods in terms of consistency and quality. Our generated results can be viewed in the supplementary materials.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a resource-efficient method for text-to-video generation using dynamic mask guidance.  It addresses challenges like high training costs, data requirements, and inconsistencies in foreground object motion in existing diffusion-based video generation models.  The proposed approach uses a foreground mask branch within the network to improve text-position matching and motion control. It also utilizes a first-frame sharing strategy and autoregressive extension for more stable and longer video generation.  Experiments demonstrate improved consistency and quality compared to previous methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a relatively novel approach to controlled video generation, and the focus on resource efficiency is a definite strength. The core idea of using mask-guided training for better foreground control is interesting and potentially valuable. The explicit incorporation of a mask branch and the mask cross-attention mechanism offer tangible ways to improve performance. The first frame sharing strategy and autoregressive extension are sensible choices for addressing temporal consistency.

Here's a breakdown of its strengths and weaknesses:

**Strengths:**

*   **Resource Efficiency:** Explicitly addresses a significant practical barrier in video generation research (high computational cost). Training on a single GPU and using a small dataset is a strong selling point.
*   **Controllability:** Provides a more direct and intuitive control mechanism over video generation compared to solely relying on text prompts, enhancing user interaction and editing capabilities.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** Identifies and tackles specific problems with existing methods, such as foreground object disappearance and unnatural foreground/background fusion.
*   **Quantitative and Qualitative Results:**  Presents both types of results, which strengthens the claims made. Shows improvements in consistency and quality compared to baselines, including user preference.
*   **Ablation study:** Validates the impact of specific components of the proposed architecture.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The first-frame approach and autoregressive extension are quite standard approaches in this field. While they contribute to the overall performance, they are not the core innovation.
*   **Dataset Dependency:** While the authors claim resource efficiency, the method could be highly dependent on the characteristics of their specific, small datasets.  It's not clear if the method would generalize well to more complex and diverse scenarios.
*   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses mostly on improvements in consistency and foreground control. It does not significantly explore novel creative video generation, for example, complex scene transitions, or more expressive artistic styles.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** The evaluation metrics are good, but there is no evaluation of the generated video to real video. Metrics used are between generated videos. This is a huge flaw in this paper and needs addressing if the authors want the paper to be taken seriously.

**Significance:**

The work has the potential to lower the barrier to entry for research in video generation.  If the resource efficiency claims hold true, it could allow more researchers and practitioners to experiment with and develop new video generation techniques.  The focus on controllability also has practical implications for video editing and content creation.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a practical contribution to the field by addressing resource constraints in text-to-video generation.  The mask-guided approach shows promise, but the limited novelty of some components, possible dataset dependency, and narrow scope of impact hold it back. The lack of human evaluation for video generated vs real video is a huge flaw in the paper.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 5/10

### PDDM: Pseudo Depth Diffusion Model for RGB-PD Semantic Segmentation Based in Complex Indoor Scenes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18393v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhua Xu, Hong Liu, Jianbing Wu, Jinfu Liu
- **Abstract**: The integration of RGB and depth modalities significantly enhances the accuracy of segmenting complex indoor scenes, with depth data from RGB-D cameras playing a crucial role in this improvement. However, collecting an RGB-D dataset is more expensive than an RGB dataset due to the need for specialized depth sensors. Aligning depth and RGB images also poses challenges due to sensor positioning and issues like missing data and noise. In contrast, Pseudo Depth (PD) from high-precision depth estimation algorithms can eliminate the dependence on RGB-D sensors and alignment processes, as well as provide effective depth information and show significant potential in semantic segmentation. Therefore, to explore the practicality of utilizing pseudo depth instead of real depth for semantic segmentation, we design an RGB-PD segmentation pipeline to integrate RGB and pseudo depth and propose a Pseudo Depth Aggregation Module (PDAM) for fully exploiting the informative clues provided by the diverse pseudo depth maps. The PDAM aggregates multiple pseudo depth maps into a single modality, making it easily adaptable to other RGB-D segmentation methods. In addition, the pre-trained diffusion model serves as a strong feature extractor for RGB segmentation tasks, but multi-modal diffusion-based segmentation methods remain unexplored. Therefore, we present a Pseudo Depth Diffusion Model (PDDM) that adopts a large-scale text-image diffusion model as a feature extractor and a simple yet effective fusion strategy to integrate pseudo depth. To verify the applicability of pseudo depth and our PDDM, we perform extensive experiments on the NYUv2 and SUNRGB-D datasets. The experimental results demonstrate that pseudo depth can effectively enhance segmentation performance, and our PDDM achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming other methods by +6.98 mIoU on NYUv2 and +2.11 mIoU on SUNRGB-D.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper and will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for RGB-D semantic segmentation using pseudo-depth (PD) maps generated from RGB images instead of relying on real depth data from RGB-D cameras. The core contributions are: (1) A Pseudo Depth Aggregation Module (PDAM) designed to effectively fuse multiple PD maps generated by different depth estimation algorithms into a single, more robust PD representation. This module employs channel and spatial attention mechanisms to weigh the contributions of different PD sources. (2) A Pseudo Depth Diffusion Model (PDDM), which leverages a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model as a feature extractor and introduces a simple yet effective fusion strategy to integrate the aggregated PD information.  The PD information is incorporated as a structured noise in the diffusion process. Extensive experiments on NYUv2 and SUNRGB-D datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method, achieving state-of-the-art performance and showing that PD maps can indeed significantly enhance semantic segmentation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty in exploiting Pseudo Depth:** The paper effectively addresses the limitation and cost associated with real-depth sensors by utilizing pseudo-depth generated from readily available RGB images. The focus on replacing real depth entirely with *multiple* pseudo-depth estimates is relatively novel in the RGB-D segmentation domain, offering a more accessible and flexible approach.
    *   **PDAM Effectiveness:** The proposed PDAM module appears to be a significant contribution, demonstrating the ability to aggregate information from different PD maps effectively. The ablation studies in the paper support this claim.
    *   **Integration with Diffusion Models:** Incorporating pseudo-depth into a diffusion-based segmentation framework is a novel approach.  Treating the pseudo depth as structured noise and integrating it early in the diffusion process is interesting and avoids expensive dual branches.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results are compelling, showcasing state-of-the-art performance on standard benchmarks. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the proposed framework.
    *   **Code Availability:** Code availability enhances reproducibility and adoption by the research community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The approach heavily relies on pre-trained diffusion models (Stable Diffusion) and depth estimation models. While this is a common practice, it may limit the transferability or adaptability of the method to scenarios where suitable pre-trained models are not available. The paper doesn't explore the sensitivity of their approach to the choice of these pre-trained models.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Fusion Strategies:** While the paper proposes a simple fusion strategy, it might benefit from exploring more advanced fusion mechanisms between the RGB and PD features. Early fusion, while efficient, might not be optimal for all scenarios. This is partially mitigated by their exploration of early, mid, and late fusion strategies.
    *   **Lack of detailed explanation of the limitations of early, mid, and late fusion strategies:** While they describe the effectiveness of early fusion compared to mid and late fusion strategies, the analysis regarding why the two former are less effective could be more thorough.
    *   **Dataset Bias:** Both NYUv2 and SUNRGB-D, while standard, are indoor scene datasets.  The effectiveness of the method in outdoor or other domain adaptation scenarios is unknown.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by promoting the use of pseudo-depth maps for RGB-D semantic segmentation. This approach lowers the barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners who may not have access to expensive RGB-D cameras.
    *   The demonstrated performance improvement over existing methods highlights the potential of PDAM and PDDM, especially in resource-constrained settings.
    *   The work opens up new avenues for research in semantic segmentation, exploring the potential of integrating different modalities and leveraging powerful pre-trained models.

**Score Justification:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*   **Rationale:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty in its approach to semantic segmentation using multiple pseudo-depth maps and its clever integration with diffusion models. The PDAM module is a valuable contribution and enhances the utility of pseudo-depth. The experimental results are strong and support the claims made in the paper. However, the heavy reliance on pre-trained models, limited exploration of fusion strategies, and dataset bias slightly detract from the overall impact. The lack of in-depth analysis of why the best fusion strategy worked also limits the paper's contribution. While the paper represents a worthwhile contribution, several aspects could be further improved.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Solving Situation Puzzles with Large Language Model and External Reformulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18394v1)
- **Authors**: Kun Li, Xinwei Chen, Tianyou Song, Chengrui Zhou, Zhuoran Liu, Zhenyan Zhang, Jiangjian Guo, Qing Shan
- **Abstract**: In recent years, large language models (LLMs) have shown an impressive ability to perform arithmetic and symbolic reasoning tasks. However, we found that LLMs (e.g., ChatGPT) cannot perform well on reasoning that requires multiple rounds of dialogue, especially when solving situation puzzles. Specifically, LLMs intend to ask very detailed questions focusing on a specific aspect or same/similar questions after several rounds of Q&As. To help LLMs get out of the above dilemma, we propose a novel external reformulation methodology, where the situation puzzle will be reformulated after several rounds of Q&A or when the LLMs raise an incorrect guess. Experiments show superior performance (e.g., win rate, number of question/guess attempts) of our method than directly using LLMs for solving situation puzzles, highlighting the potential of strategic problem reformulation to enhance the reasoning capabilities of LLMs in complex interactive scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of Large Language Models (LLMs) struggling with situation puzzles, which require multiple rounds of dialogue and reasoning. The authors observe that LLMs tend to get stuck, asking detailed or repetitive questions. To overcome this, they propose integrating LLMs with external reformulation. This involves reformulating the situation puzzle description using previous question-and-answer pairs as hints, essentially resetting the LLM's reasoning process. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that this reformulation method leads to superior performance compared to directly using LLMs for solving these puzzles, showing improvements in win rate and a reduction in the number of question/guess attempts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of reformulating the puzzle description with hints derived from past interactions is somewhat novel in the context of LLMs and situation puzzles. While prompt reformulation has been explored in other NLP tasks, applying it iteratively within a multi-turn dialogue setting to specifically address the LLM's tendency to get stuck is a valuable contribution. However, the connection between the questions and the reformulated version of the situation is unclear. The selection method for choosing which questions to use during the reformulation phase is simple. It does not evaluate the relevance to the task and just relies on their answers (Yes/No/Irrelevant).
*   **Significance:** The problem that the paper addresses is significant. While LLMs have shown impressive capabilities in various tasks, they often lack robust reasoning skills, especially in scenarios that require deep, multi-step inference. Improving the ability of LLMs to handle situation puzzles represents a step towards enhancing their general reasoning capabilities and their ability to engage in more meaningful dialogues. By making it solve the problem more laterally, LLMs can become useful at solving similar reasoning-based tasks.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper identifies a specific limitation of LLMs in a well-defined task (situation puzzles).
    *   The proposed reformulation method is intuitive and relatively easy to implement.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in performance compared to the baseline, suggesting the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The paper provides a clear formulation of the problem and the proposed solution.
    *   The work considers a challenging class of problems that push the reasoning capabilities of LLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The mechanism of generating hints from Q&A pairs could be more sophisticated. The current approach simply incorporates these Q&As in the new description without understanding the relevance of the hint to finding a solution to the puzzles. A more semantically aware or importance-weighted approach could be beneficial.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a relatively small set of 5 situation puzzles, which may not be representative of the broader class of such puzzles. Expanding the dataset would improve the robustness of the findings.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of *why* the reformulation method works. What specific cognitive bottlenecks are being addressed by providing the LLM with these hints? A more theoretical justification would strengthen the paper.
    *   The selection of K and M parameter values lack strong justification. A broader ablation study of these parameters and their effect on results would add value to the paper.
    *   The related work section doesn't discuss the limits of prompt engineering, e.g., the maximum input length and possible information loss in long contexts.
    *   The novelty of the method relies on the insight that a reformulated prompt helps LLMs come up with a solution. While true, it does not provide in-depth insight into why this happens or how one can optimally reformulate the problem.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper could potentially influence research on improving the reasoning abilities of LLMs, particularly in interactive settings. The reformulation method could be adapted and applied to other tasks that require multi-step inference and dialogue, like complex problem-solving or planning. If the approach proves to be generalizable and scalable, it could have a tangible impact on the development of more robust and intelligent conversational AI systems.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and pragmatic approach to a relevant problem and demonstrates positive results. The limitations outlined above (hint generation, small evaluation set, lack of in-depth analysis, parameter tuning) prevents it from scoring higher. Despite its shortcomings, it introduces a useful technique for improving the reasoning capabilities of LLMs, which is expected to encourage similar work in the community.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Instruct-CLIP: Improving Instruction-Guided Image Editing with Automated Data Refinement Using Contrastive Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18406v1)
- **Authors**: Sherry X. Chen, Misha Sra, Pradeep Sen
- **Abstract**: Although natural language instructions offer an intuitive way to guide automated image editing, deep-learning models often struggle to achieve high-quality results, largely due to challenges in creating large, high-quality training datasets. Previous work has typically relied on text-toimage (T2I) generative models to produce pairs of original and edited images that simulate the input/output of an instruction-guided image-editing model. However, these image pairs often fail to align with the specified edit instructions due to the limitations of T2I models, which negatively impacts models trained on such datasets. To address this, we present Instruct-CLIP, a self-supervised method that learns the semantic changes between original and edited images to refine and better align the instructions in existing datasets. Furthermore, we adapt Instruct-CLIP to handle noisy latent images and diffusion timesteps so that it can be used to train latent diffusion models (LDMs) [19] and efficiently enforce alignment between the edit instruction and the image changes in latent space at any step of the diffusion pipeline. We use Instruct-CLIP to correct the InstructPix2Pix dataset and get over 120K refined samples we then use to fine-tune their model, guided by our novel Instruct-CLIP-based loss function. The resulting model can produce edits that are more aligned with the given instructions. Our code and dataset are available at https://github.com/SherryXTChen/Instruct-CLIP.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Instruct-CLIP paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Instruct-CLIP, a self-supervised method to improve instruction-guided image editing. It tackles the problem of noisy or misaligned instruction-image pairs in existing datasets, which hinders the training of high-quality image editing models. Instruct-CLIP learns semantic changes between original and edited images to refine and better align existing instructions. It uses a contrastive learning approach to embed image pairs and instructions into a shared feature space. Furthermore, the model handles noisy latent images and diffusion timesteps to train latent diffusion models (LDMs) for efficient enforcement of alignment in latent space. The method refines the InstructPix2Pix dataset, generates refined samples, and utilizes an Instruct-CLIP-based loss function during fine-tuning.  The improved model demonstrates better alignment between edits and instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using contrastive learning to refine instructions in an image editing dataset is relatively novel.  It's an intelligent way to leverage existing, imperfect datasets without requiring massive manual annotation. Adapting this to the latent space of diffusion models to influence training directly is another clever extension. The dual-purpose aspect of the model (refining data and enhancing training) is a positive.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a practical bottleneck in instruction-guided image editing: the availability of high-quality training data. By providing a method to improve existing datasets, the paper can enable the development of more effective and reliable image editing tools.  The improved performance shown over the baseline InstructPix2Pix is promising. The creation and release of the refined dataset is a valuable contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is self-supervised, reducing reliance on manual annotation.
    *   It is adaptable to the latent space of diffusion models for efficient training.
    *   The contrastive learning approach for instruction refinement is a smart way to leverage existing data.
    *   The experiments show improved performance compared to a strong baseline.
    *   Release of the refined dataset promotes further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on CLIP and existing T2I models means the method is still limited by their capabilities.  It can't correct errors fundamentally beyond the capacity of these base models. The results shown in the failure cases support this.
    *   While the quantitative metrics show improvement, the authors admit these aren't perfect indicators of visual quality, underscoring a general challenge in this field.
    *   The user study provides strong support, but there's still a degree of subjectivity inherent in assessing image editing quality.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in instruction-guided image editing by:
    *   Providing a method for creating better training datasets.
    *   Encouraging the development of loss functions that directly enforce instruction alignment in latent space.
    *   Serving as a benchmark for future instruction refinement methods.
    *   Facilitating improved image editing tools that better adhere to user instructions.

*   **Justification for Score:**  The Instruct-CLIP approach is a valuable contribution. It provides a practical solution to a real-world problem of noisy datasets in instruction-guided editing. The paper presents a technically sound approach, thorough experimentation, and clear presentation of results, and includes helpful release of the dataset to promote further use. While the core ideas build on CLIP and diffusion models, the specific application and adaptation for instruction refinement represent a non-trivial advance. The results of user studies and other experiments support claims of improvement with dataset re-refinement. But in terms of novelty it doesn't really do a lot of groundbreaking work that radically advances the field, so a score of 7 seems appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Panorama Generation From NFoV Image Done Right
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18420v1)
- **Authors**: Dian Zheng, Cheng Zhang, Xiao-Ming Wu, Cao Li, Chengfei Lv, Jian-Fang Hu, Wei-Shi Zheng
- **Abstract**: Generating 360-degree panoramas from narrow field of view (NFoV) image is a promising computer vision task for Virtual Reality (VR) applications. Existing methods mostly assess the generated panoramas with InceptionNet or CLIP based metrics, which tend to perceive the image quality and is \textbf{not suitable for evaluating the distortion}. In this work, we first propose a distortion-specific CLIP, named Distort-CLIP to accurately evaluate the panorama distortion and discover the \textbf{``visual cheating''} phenomenon in previous works (\ie, tending to improve the visual results by sacrificing distortion accuracy). This phenomenon arises because prior methods employ a single network to learn the distinct panorama distortion and content completion at once, which leads the model to prioritize optimizing the latter. To address the phenomenon, we propose \textbf{PanoDecouple}, a decoupled diffusion model framework, which decouples the panorama generation into distortion guidance and content completion, aiming to generate panoramas with both accurate distortion and visual appeal. Specifically, we design a DistortNet for distortion guidance by imposing panorama-specific distortion prior and a modified condition registration mechanism; and a ContentNet for content completion by imposing perspective image information. Additionally, a distortion correction loss function with Distort-CLIP is introduced to constrain the distortion explicitly. The extensive experiments validate that PanoDecouple surpasses existing methods both in distortion and visual metrics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of generating 360-degree panoramas from narrow field-of-view (NFoV) images.  It identifies a "visual cheating" phenomenon in existing methods, where improving visual quality comes at the expense of distortion accuracy. This is attributed to single models learning both distortion and content completion simultaneously, prioritizing content.  To counteract this, the authors propose PanoDecouple, a decoupled diffusion model framework.  PanoDecouple separates the task into distortion guidance (using a DistortNet with a novel distortion map representation and modified condition registration) and content completion (using a ContentNet leveraging perspective image information). A distortion correction loss with a newly proposed distortion-specific CLIP (Distort-CLIP) is introduced to further constrain distortion.  Experiments demonstrate improved distortion and visual metrics compared to existing methods, even with significantly less training data. The paper also shows applications to text editing and text-to-image panorama generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Distort-CLIP:** The idea of a distortion-specific CLIP model is novel and addresses a real problem with existing evaluation metrics. The construction and contrastive learning mechanism for Distort-CLIP appears sound. This is a key contribution.
*   **PanoDecouple Framework:** Decoupling distortion guidance and content completion is a logical approach to addressing the "visual cheating" problem. The design of DistortNet and ContentNet, while building upon existing diffusion models and ControlNet, incorporates novel elements like the panorama-specific distortion prior, Taylor expansion positional encoding, and the modified condition registration.
*   **Condition Registration Mechanism Modification:** Modifying ControlNet to inject the position-encoding like distortion map into all U-Net blocks (rather than just the first) appears to be an important design choice that allows for better incorporation of positional information throughout the network.
*   **Distortion Correction Loss:** Augmenting the existing framework with a distortion correction loss based on the Distort-CLIP model further enhances distortion accuracy.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Real Problem:** The paper tackles a significant limitation in existing panorama generation methods. The "visual cheating" phenomenon identified by the authors is compelling, and their solution demonstrates a clear improvement.
*   **Improved Metrics:**  Highlighting and addressing the inadequacy of commonly used metrics like FID in capturing distortion is crucial. The Distort-FID metric provides a more relevant evaluation, leading to more meaningful comparisons.
*   **Data Efficiency:**  The fact that PanoDecouple achieves superior results with considerably less training data is significant.  It suggests that the decoupled approach is more efficient and may generalize better.
*   **Practical Applications:** The successful applications to text editing and text-to-image generation demonstrate the versatility of the proposed approach.

**Strengths:**

*   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
*   Novel and technically sound solutions (Distort-CLIP, decoupled framework, condition registration modification).
*   Strong experimental results with quantitative and qualitative evaluations.
*   Demonstrated practical applications.
*   Clear writing and well-organized presentation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** While leveraging pre-trained models is a common practice, the paper doesn't fully explore the potential limitations or biases introduced by these models.  A deeper analysis of how the pre-trained models affect the final results would be beneficial.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While novel, the core technology builds heavily upon existing work in diffusion models and ControlNet. The improvements are significant, but still incremental. The impact could be lower due to this incremental improvement.
*   **Limited Dataset Exploration:** The main datasets used in the experiments are Sun360 and Laval Indoor. The authors could increase the impact of the paper by showcasing the results on additional benchmark panorama datasets.

**Justification of Score:**

PanoDecouple presents a solid and well-executed solution to a relevant problem in panorama generation. The Distort-CLIP metric and decoupled framework are key contributions, addressing the "visual cheating" phenomenon effectively. The data efficiency and practical applications add to the value of the work. However, the reliance on existing diffusion models and ControlNet, with the resulting improvements mainly incremental, limits the overall impact of this paper. Also, a more comprehensive dataset exploration would further increase the impact of the paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Breaking the Encoder Barrier for Seamless Video-Language Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18422v1)
- **Authors**: Handong Li, Yiyuan Zhang, Longteng Guo, Xiangyu Yue, Jing Liu
- **Abstract**: Most Video-Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) adopt an encoder-decoder framework, where a vision encoder extracts frame-wise features for processing by a language model. However, this approach incurs high computational costs, introduces resolution biases, and struggles to capture fine-grained multimodal interactions. To overcome these limitations, we propose ELVA, an encoder-free Video-LLM that directly models nuanced video-language interactions without relying on a vision encoder. ELVA employs token merging to construct a bottom-up hierarchical representation and incorporates a video guidance supervisor for direct spatiotemporal representation learning. Additionally, a hybrid-resolution mechanism strategically integrates high- and low-resolution frames as inputs to achieve an optimal balance between performance and efficiency. With only 7M publicly available video-text pairs, ELVA achieves performance on par with encoder-based Video-LLMs while reducing FLOPs by up to 95\% and inference latency by 92\%, offering a scalable and efficient solution for real-time video understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ELVA, an Encoder-free Large Video Language Model (Video-LLM). ELVA aims to address the limitations of traditional encoder-decoder Video-LLMs, which suffer from high computational costs, resolution biases, and difficulties in capturing fine-grained multimodal interactions. ELVA achieves this by directly modeling video-language interactions without a separate vision encoder. It employs token merging for hierarchical representation learning, a video guidance supervisor for spatiotemporal representation learning, and a hybrid-resolution mechanism to balance performance and efficiency. The authors demonstrate that ELVA achieves performance comparable to encoder-based models while significantly reducing computational costs (FLOPs and inference latency).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the encoder-free architecture for Video-LLMs. This deviates from the standard encoder-decoder structure prevalent in the field. The individual components (token merging, video guidance, hybrid resolution) are not entirely new concepts in isolation, but their combination and application within an encoder-free framework constitute a significant innovation. The use of video guidance as supervision offers a novel way for spatial-temporal representation learning. The progressive pre-training strategy is also a notable contribution.

*   **Significance:** ELVA's potential significance is high. The demonstrated reduction in computational cost and latency could make real-time video understanding more feasible. The encoder-free design addresses limitations related to resolution biases and multimodal interaction bottlenecks inherent in encoder-based methods. The authors also provided detailed analysis on how different components of ELVA impact its performance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Significant computational efficiency gains (FLOPs, latency).
    *   Competitive performance relative to encoder-based models with similar capacity.
    *   Ablation studies clearly demonstrate the impact of various components.
    *   Addresses important limitations of existing Video-LLMs.
    *   Progressive training paradigm allows for effective spatial-temporal representation learning.
    *   Hybrid resolution inference is a great trade off between performance and compute.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a pre-trained SigLIP model for video guidance, although effective, introduces a form of dependency on external models. While not as computationally expensive as a full encoder, it might still present a constraint.
    *   The experimental setup, while thorough, primarily relies on existing benchmark datasets. Evaluation on more challenging, real-world video understanding tasks could further solidify its impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** If ELVA's performance and efficiency translate to other video understanding tasks, it could drive a shift towards encoder-free architectures in the Video-LLM field. This could lead to more scalable and adaptable models for various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially impactful architecture for Video-LLMs. The encoder-free design offers several advantages over existing approaches, and the experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in computational efficiency without sacrificing performance. While some components build upon existing ideas, their combination and application in this context are innovative.

Despite its strengths, there are some weaknesses. The dependence on a pre-trained video understanding model for the video guidance supervisor prevents ELVA from being truly encoder-free.
However, the potential for ELVA to influence the field, especially in enabling more scalable and efficient Video-LLMs, is considerable.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Simple yet Effective Layout Token in Large Language Models for Document Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18434v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaoqing Zhu, Chuwei Luo, Zirui Shao, Feiyu Gao, Hangdi Xing, Qi Zheng, Ji Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent methods that integrate spatial layouts with text for document understanding in large language models (LLMs) have shown promising results. A commonly used method is to represent layout information as text tokens and interleave them with text content as inputs to the LLMs. However, such a method still demonstrates limitations, as it requires additional position IDs for tokens that are used to represent layout information. Due to the constraint on max position IDs, assigning them to layout information reduces those available for text content, reducing the capacity for the model to learn from the text during training, while also introducing a large number of potentially untrained position IDs during long-context inference, which can hinder performance on document understanding tasks. To address these issues, we propose LayTokenLLM, a simple yet effective method for document understanding. LayTokenLLM represents layout information as a single token per text segment and uses a specialized positional encoding scheme. It shares position IDs between text and layout tokens, eliminating the need for additional position IDs. This design maintains the model's capacity to learn from text while mitigating long-context issues during inference. Furthermore, a novel pre-training objective called Next Interleaved Text and Layout Token Prediction (NTLP) is devised to enhance cross-modality learning between text and layout tokens. Extensive experiments show that LayTokenLLM outperforms existing layout-integrated LLMs and MLLMs of similar scales on multi-page document understanding tasks, as well as most single-page tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LayTokenLLM, a method for integrating layout information into large language models (LLMs) for document understanding. Unlike previous approaches that represent layout information as additional text tokens requiring extra position IDs, LayTokenLLM represents the layout as a single token per text segment and shares position IDs between text and layout tokens. This eliminates the need for additional position IDs, preserving the model's capacity to learn from text and mitigating long-context issues during inference. The paper also proposes a novel pre-training objective, Next Interleaved Text and Layout Token Prediction (NTLP), to enhance cross-modality learning. Experiments on multi-page document understanding tasks demonstrate that LayTokenLLM outperforms existing layout-integrated LLMs and MLLMs of similar scales, and achieves competitive or superior performance on single-page tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the simplified and efficient integration of layout information into LLMs. Sharing position IDs between text and layout tokens, and the compression of the layout into a single token, are relatively novel approaches. The NTLP pre-training objective, which promotes cross-modal prediction is a welcome addition. Compared to LayoutLM, which uses extra parameters for layout encoding, LayTokenLLM uses the trainable projector and position encoding to encode the layout information.

*   **Significance:** Document understanding is a crucial area, and efficiently leveraging layout information in LLMs is an important goal. The significance of this work lies in its ability to achieve better performance on complex, multi-page documents while addressing the limitations of existing methods, such as the reduction of the available position IDs and introduction of unlearned position IDs. If validated and expanded upon, LayTokenLLM offers a more practical approach for document understanding with LLMs, especially regarding memory and pre-training data requirements.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** LayTokenLLM is more efficient in terms of position ID utilization compared to prior Layout-as-Token methods, leading to better learning of text content and improved handling of long documents.
    *   **Performance:**  The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over other methods, particularly on multi-page document understanding tasks.
    *   **Simplicity:** The proposed architecture is relatively simple to implement and doesn’t require substantial modifications to the underlying LLM architecture.
    *   **Cross-modal Learning:** The Next Interleaved Text and Layout Token Prediction objective(NTLP) enables the Layout-as-Token architecture to enhance the cross-modal prediction and relation learning between text and layout.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper doesn't address other graphical elements, such as charts and icons. The layout information is only limited to the bounding box.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:**  The results might be highly dependent on the pre-training data and the specific benchmark datasets used. More comprehensive experiments on a wider variety of document types and tasks are needed to fully validate the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Ablation study:** Although the Ablation study shows the effectiveness of Layout Token and NTLP, it is worth to compare the Layout Tokenizer with other trainable encoder to prove the efficiency of LayTokenLLM.

*   **Potential Impact:** LayTokenLLM has the potential to influence the field by providing a more practical and scalable approach for integrating layout information in LLMs. Its simplicity and efficiency could lead to wider adoption and further research in this area. Also, by integrating spatial layout information with the LLMs, LayTokenLLM is better used in the industrial domain that need the ability for document understanding.

*   **Justification of Score:** While the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field, it is not without limitations. The novelty is incremental but significant in addressing a specific problem (position ID limitation). The performance gains are promising, especially on multi-page document tasks. However, the scope is limited, and further validation on more diverse datasets and tasks is necessary to fully assess its generalizability. Therefore, a score of 7 is warranted, reflecting its meaningful contribution but acknowledging the need for more extensive evaluation and expansion of its capabilities.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Latent Space Super-Resolution for Higher-Resolution Image Generation with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18446v1)
- **Authors**: Jinho Jeong, Sangmin Han, Jinwoo Kim, Seon Joo Kim
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we propose LSRNA, a novel framework for higher-resolution (exceeding 1K) image generation using diffusion models by leveraging super-resolution directly in the latent space. Existing diffusion models struggle with scaling beyond their training resolutions, often leading to structural distortions or content repetition. Reference-based methods address the issues by upsampling a low-resolution reference to guide higher-resolution generation. However, they face significant challenges: upsampling in latent space often causes manifold deviation, which degrades output quality. On the other hand, upsampling in RGB space tends to produce overly smoothed outputs. To overcome these limitations, LSRNA combines Latent space Super-Resolution (LSR) for manifold alignment and Region-wise Noise Addition (RNA) to enhance high-frequency details. Our extensive experiments demonstrate that integrating LSRNA outperforms state-of-the-art reference-based methods across various resolutions and metrics, while showing the critical role of latent space upsampling in preserving detail and sharpness. The code is available at https://github.com/3587jjh/LSRNA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LSRNA (Latent Space Super-Resolution with Region-wise Noise Addition), a novel framework designed to improve high-resolution image generation using diffusion models.  LSRNA addresses challenges in scaling diffusion models beyond their training resolutions, which often leads to structural distortions or content repetition. The core idea is to perform super-resolution directly in the latent space, combining Latent space Super-Resolution (LSR) for manifold alignment and Region-wise Noise Addition (RNA) to enhance high-frequency details.  LSRNA improves on existing reference-based methods that either suffer from manifold deviation due to naive latent space upsampling or overly smooth results from RGB space upsampling. The authors integrate LSRNA with existing methods like DemoFusion and Pixelsmith and demonstrate its effectiveness across various resolutions and metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel combination of techniques rather than groundbreaking invention. The key idea of performing super-resolution in the latent space is not entirely new, but the specific combination of LSR (learning to map low-resolution to higher-resolution latent spaces) and RNA (region-adaptive noise injection) is where the novelty lies.  The learning of the latent space super-resolution is likely the strongest novel aspect. The contribution is more about a clever engineering approach to improving existing methods than a fundamentally new theoretical insight. The integration of these modules is well-executed, though arguably incremental in nature.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work comes from addressing a practical and important problem: generating high-resolution images with diffusion models without training from scratch or suffering from common artifacts.  The improvements demonstrated over existing methods (DemoFusion, Pixelsmith) are meaningful, both quantitatively (metrics) and qualitatively (visual quality, runtime).  The framework's ability to speed up generation while enhancing detail makes it practically valuable.  It has the potential to influence future research in high-resolution image synthesis with diffusion models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed LSR and RNA modules.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation (quantitative and qualitative) across different resolutions.
    *   Ablation studies that justify the design choices.
    *   Integration with existing methods to demonstrate generalizability.
    *   Address the challenging issue of manifold deviation during latent space upsampling.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The components (LSR and RNA) are not revolutionary individually but a combination of established techniques.
    *   The improvements, while significant, are incremental compared to the base methods (DemoFusion, Pixelsmith).
    *   The method relies on a pre-trained LDM, limiting its generation ability to the capacity of that LDM.
    *   The limitation regarding the RNA strength's dependency on the integrated method and noise schedule is a notable drawback.
    *   The training of the LSR module relies on real-world dataset which does not fully correspond to domain of diffusion models.
*   **Impact:** The work presents a practical technique and could have a medium impact. Future work could build upon this by exploring better architectures for the LSR and RNA modules, or integrating this within end-to-end training. While there are limitations in the approach, it presents a framework for future work, and has immediate applications in high resolution synthesis.

**Justification for Score:**

LSRNA makes a solid contribution to the field of high-resolution image generation with diffusion models. While it doesn't introduce entirely new theoretical concepts, it cleverly combines existing techniques to address practical limitations in existing methods. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of LSRNA in improving image quality and generation speed. The limitations, while present, are clearly acknowledged by the authors. The code and approach would be valuable for researchers. Therefore, the paper is deserving of a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### InPO: Inversion Preference Optimization with Reparametrized DDIM for Efficient Diffusion Model Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18454v1)
- **Authors**: Yunhong Lu, Qichao Wang, Hengyuan Cao, Xierui Wang, Xiaoyin Xu, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Without using explicit reward, direct preference optimization (DPO) employs paired human preference data to fine-tune generative models, a method that has garnered considerable attention in large language models (LLMs). However, exploration of aligning text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models with human preferences remains limited. In comparison to supervised fine-tuning, existing methods that align diffusion model suffer from low training efficiency and subpar generation quality due to the long Markov chain process and the intractability of the reverse process. To address these limitations, we introduce DDIM-InPO, an efficient method for direct preference alignment of diffusion models. Our approach conceptualizes diffusion model as a single-step generative model, allowing us to fine-tune the outputs of specific latent variables selectively. In order to accomplish this objective, we first assign implicit rewards to any latent variable directly via a reparameterization technique. Then we construct an Inversion technique to estimate appropriate latent variables for preference optimization. This modification process enables the diffusion model to only fine-tune the outputs of latent variables that have a strong correlation with the preference dataset. Experimental results indicate that our DDIM-InPO achieves state-of-the-art performance with just 400 steps of fine-tuning, surpassing all preference aligning baselines for T2I diffusion models in human preference evaluation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "InPO: Inversion Preference Optimization with Reparametrized DDIM for Efficient Diffusion Model Alignment" introduces a novel method, DDIM-InPO, to align text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models with human preferences more efficiently.  Instead of treating the diffusion process as a long Markov chain, they reframe it as a single-step generative model.  This allows them to selectively fine-tune the outputs of specific latent variables that are directly linked to the target image.  They use a reparameterization technique to allocate implicit rewards to these latent variables and an inversion technique to estimate appropriate latent variables for preference optimization, mitigating the effects of sparse rewards.  The authors demonstrate through experiments that DDIM-InPO achieves state-of-the-art performance with significantly fewer fine-tuning steps compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in reframing the diffusion process and using DDIM inversion specifically for preference optimization in T2I models. The combination of reparameterizing the diffusion model as a single-step generative model with inversion to target specific latent variables directly is a contribution. While previous methods have explored DPO for diffusion models, the specific technique of using inversion and targeting latent variable outputs seems relatively unique.

*   **Significance:**  Efficiency in aligning T2I models is a significant problem. Methods that require excessive computation or data are less practical.  The claim of achieving state-of-the-art performance with just 400 fine-tuning steps directly addresses this. If the results are replicable and robust, it has the potential to significantly reduce the computational burden of aligning diffusion models. The ablation studies provide insights into the specific components contributing to the improvement, increasing the practical significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly defines the problem and motivates the need for a more efficient alignment method.
    *   The approach is well-explained and supported by relevant equations and derivations.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate a significant improvement in training efficiency and generation quality compared to existing methods.
    *   The qualitative results further support the claims by showcasing the enhanced visual appeal and prompt alignment of the generated images.
    * The additional materials present in the supplementary material, especially the discussion of the dataset's limitations is a great touch

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies on the Pick-a-Pic v2 dataset, which is acknowledged to have potential biases and limitations (e.g., presence of harmful content). The dependence on this dataset somewhat limits the generalizability of the results and raises ethical considerations, necessitating a more thorough sensitivity analysis or validation on other datasets.
    *   The ablation studies could be more extensive. Specifically, varying the number of inversion steps further and analyzing their impact on both efficiency and performance in finer detail would reinforce the claims better.
    * The method is offline fine-tuning, and has limitations regarding dataset quality and is susceptible to harmful content.
    * There are certain claims in the text that feel overconfident and the paper could benefit from a more cautious approach.

*   **Impact:** If validated by other researchers, this work could become a standard technique for aligning T2I diffusion models, especially in resource-constrained settings. It opens avenues for future research into exploring different inversion techniques, latent variable selection strategies, and reinforcement learning approaches for preference optimization.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of T2I model alignment. The proposed method offers a tangible improvement in training efficiency without sacrificing generation quality. The paper is well-written, and the experimental results provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the approach. However, the dependence on a potentially biased dataset and some unproven claims and overconfident language slightly lower the score.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates clear novelty and tackles a significant problem with a promising solution. The method's efficiency is a substantial advantage. However, the reliance on a single, potentially biased dataset and minor unaddressed issues/concerns limit the overall score. Additional validation and a more extensive analysis of the limitations would significantly increase the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hiding Images in Diffusion Models by Editing Learned Score Functions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18459v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyu Chen, Yunqiao Yang, Nan Zhong, Kede Ma
- **Abstract**: Hiding data using neural networks (i.e., neural steganography) has achieved remarkable success across both discriminative classifiers and generative adversarial networks. However, the potential of data hiding in diffusion models remains relatively unexplored. Current methods exhibit limitations in achieving high extraction accuracy, model fidelity, and hiding efficiency due primarily to the entanglement of the hiding and extraction processes with multiple denoising diffusion steps. To address these, we describe a simple yet effective approach that embeds images at specific timesteps in the reverse diffusion process by editing the learned score functions. Additionally, we introduce a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that combines gradient-based parameter selection with low-rank adaptation to enhance model fidelity and hiding efficiency. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that our method extracts high-quality images at human-indistinguishable levels, replicates the original model behaviors at both sample and population levels, and embeds images orders of magnitude faster than prior methods. Besides, our method naturally supports multi-recipient scenarios through independent extraction channels.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new method for hiding images within diffusion models by directly editing the learned score functions at specific timesteps of the reverse diffusion process. The key idea is to embed the secret image at a strategically chosen timestep using a secret key to generate Gaussian noise.  The approach avoids the limitations of previous methods that require retraining or fine-tuning the entire model.  To further improve model fidelity and efficiency, the authors employ a hybrid parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) method that combines gradient-based parameter selection and low-rank adaptation (LoRA).  The method is evaluated extensively, showing high extraction accuracy, preserved model fidelity, and faster embedding compared to prior works. It also demonstrates scalability to multi-recipient scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to data hiding within diffusion models.  The direct editing of score functions at a single timestep, guided by a secret key, is a significant departure from previous methods that modify the entire reverse diffusion process or train separate diffusion processes.  The application of PEFT to fine-tune the model specifically for data hiding, further improving model fidelity and efficiency, adds another layer of novelty. The multi-recipient application is also a valuable extension.

**Significance:**  The paper offers a practical solution to several key challenges in data hiding with diffusion models:

*   **Improved Accuracy:**  The demonstrated extraction accuracy (PSNR) surpasses existing methods by a considerable margin, making the method more viable for practical applications.
*   **Model Fidelity:**  The method effectively preserves the original model's generative capabilities, minimizing the risk of detection through statistical analysis.
*   **Hiding Efficiency:**  The single-timestep editing greatly reduces the computational cost of embedding images, making it more accessible and scalable than previous approaches.
*   **Scalability:** The multi-recipient functionality has practical significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear and Concise Explanation:** The paper provides a clear explanation of the method, its advantages, and its implementation.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The extensive experiments cover various aspects of the method, including extraction accuracy, model fidelity, hiding efficiency, and scalability. The comparison against existing methods is thorough and convincing.
*   **Strong Results:** The quantitative and qualitative results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method over existing techniques.
*   **Practical Contributions:** The method addresses key limitations of prior work, making it more practical and useful in real-world scenarios.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Secret Key Security:** The paper does not address the security of the secret key itself and assumes it's securely transmitted. While this is standard in steganography, explicitly mentioning it is critical.
*   **Limited Robustness Analysis:** While the paper examines the robustness to image noise, a more comprehensive analysis considering different attacks on the diffusion model or steganography detection methods would strengthen the paper. Specifically, steganalysis techniques tailored for diffusion models.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The paper demonstrates that PEFT significantly lowers the number of training parameters, there could be a more in-depth analysis discussing parameter sensitivity/stability.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a well-executed piece of research that introduces a novel and effective technique for hiding images in diffusion models.  The demonstrated improvements in accuracy, fidelity, and efficiency are substantial and address significant limitations of existing methods. The multi-recipient support further increases its practicality. While robustness analysis can be included to improve and further justify the significance of its proposed method, the technical quality of this paper and its contributions will influence the design and development of future methods and steganography.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ModiGen: A Large Language Model-Based Workflow for Multi-Task Modelica Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18460v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahui Xiang, Tong Ye, Peiyu Liu, Yinan Zhang, Wenhai Wang
- **Abstract**: Modelica is a widely adopted language for simulating complex physical systems, yet effective model creation and optimization require substantial domain expertise. Although large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated promising capabilities in code generation, their application to modeling remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we have developed benchmark datasets specifically designed to evaluate the performance of LLMs in generating Modelica component models and test cases. Our evaluation reveals substantial limitations in current LLMs, as the generated code often fails to simulate successfully. To overcome these challenges, we propose a specialized workflow that integrates supervised fine-tuning, graph retrieval-augmented generation, and feedback optimization to improve the accuracy and reliability of Modelica code generation. The evaluation results demonstrate significant performance gains: the maximum improvement in pass@1 reached 0.3349 for the component generation task and 0.2457 for the test case generation task. This research underscores the potential of LLMs to advance intelligent modeling tools and offers valuable insights for future developments in system modeling and engineering applications.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ModiGen: A Large Language Model-Based Workflow for Multi-Task Modelica Code Generation."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating Modelica code, a language used for simulating complex physical systems. While Modelica is powerful, creating and optimizing models requires significant expertise.  The authors explore the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate this process. They first create benchmark datasets for evaluating LLMs in generating Modelica component models and test cases.  Finding limitations in direct LLM application, they propose a workflow called ModiGen. ModiGen incorporates supervised fine-tuning, graph retrieval-augmented generation (GraphRAG), and feedback optimization to improve the accuracy and reliability of the generated code.  The paper demonstrates significant performance gains through experimental evaluation compared to baseline LLM performance, particularly in successfully executable Modelica code.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of LLMs to Modelica code generation is relatively novel. While there has been work on generating UML and other modeling languages, the specific challenges and opportunities presented by Modelica, with its equation-based and physical systems focus, distinguish this work. ModiGen's combination of fine-tuning, GraphRAG, and feedback optimization is a valuable contribution beyond simple prompt engineering. However, each of the individual components (fine-tuning, RAG, feedback loops) are known techniques and this reduces the inherent novelty. The novelty lies primarily in combining these techniques in a Modelica-specific workflow.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to democratize Modelica modeling. Reducing the expertise required to create and optimize models could broaden the use of Modelica across various engineering and scientific fields. The improvements in code generation accuracy and reliability are promising for accelerating the development of complex physical system simulations. The open-sourcing of datasets is a significant contribution to enabling future research. However, the limitations identified concerning complex dependencies and system-level perspectives diminish the overall significance. The potential for a democratization of Modelica code generation is a long-term vision and relies heavily on the ability to scale and generalize these methods effectively. The authors identify these limitations and propose methods to alleviate them, yet these limitations are not entirely solved and as such they reduce the overall impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Comprehensive benchmark dataset construction and open-sourcing.
    *   A novel workflow (ModiGen) incorporating multiple techniques.
    *   Quantifiable performance improvements demonstrated through experiments.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation of the proposed approach.
    *   Detailed ablation studies which demonstrate the individual contributions of various sub-components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limitations in handling complex multi-level and cross-domain dependencies. The methods do not entirely solve these limitations, which the authors acknowledge.
    *   The benchmark dataset, while well-constructed, is still limited in diversity and scale, especially for multi-physics domain modeling.
    *   Lack of a system-level perspective in the code generation task. The framework focuses on component-level code, missing holistic understanding of system architecture, limiting the scope of impact.
    *   Reliance on established techniques. The component processes of fine-tuning, RAG, and feedback optimization are well-established. The novelty primarily lies in their combination rather than entirely novel algorithms.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to influence future research in automated Modelica modeling and potentially inspire similar approaches for other domain-specific languages used in physical systems simulation. The open-sourced datasets and code will facilitate further investigation and improvements by the community.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of 7.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution by exploring the use of LLMs for Modelica code generation and providing a workflow that significantly improves upon baseline LLM performance.  The benchmark dataset and open-sourced code are valuable resources. However, the identified limitations, particularly the difficulties in handling complex dependencies and system-level architecture, temper the overall significance. The individual components are also incremental in nature. A score of 7 reflects a strong but not groundbreaking contribution to the field, with clear avenues for future work and refinement.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### MuMA: 3D PBR Texturing via Multi-Channel Multi-View Generation and Agentic Post-Processing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18461v1)
- **Authors**: Lingting Zhu, Jingrui Ye, Runze Zhang, Zeyu Hu, Yingda Yin, Lanjiong Li, Jinnan Chen, Shengju Qian, Xin Wang, Qingmin Liao, Lequan Yu
- **Abstract**: Current methods for 3D generation still fall short in physically based rendering (PBR) texturing, primarily due to limited data and challenges in modeling multi-channel materials. In this work, we propose MuMA, a method for 3D PBR texturing through Multi-channel Multi-view generation and Agentic post-processing. Our approach features two key innovations: 1) We opt to model shaded and albedo appearance channels, where the shaded channels enables the integration intrinsic decomposition modules for material properties. 2) Leveraging multimodal large language models, we emulate artists' techniques for material assessment and selection. Experiments demonstrate that MuMA achieves superior results in visual quality and material fidelity compared to existing methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "MuMA: 3D PBR Texturing via Multi-Channel Multi-View Generation and Agentic Post-Processing."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MuMA, a novel approach for generating physically based rendering (PBR) textures for 3D models.  MuMA leverages a multi-channel, multi-view diffusion model to generate shaded and albedo images, which are then fed into a material decomposition model (IDArb) to extract metallic and roughness channels. The key innovations include: (1) modeling shaded and albedo channels instead of directly predicting all PBR channels to ease training and leverage intrinsic decomposition modules; and (2) employing an agentic post-processing step using a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to score and select the best texture candidates, mimicking human artistic judgment.  The authors demonstrate superior visual quality and material fidelity compared to existing methods through qualitative and quantitative experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel combination of existing techniques, but its core innovations are incremental improvements rather than revolutionary breakthroughs.

    *   *Multi-channel, Multi-view Diffusion:* While using multi-view diffusion for 3D generation is not entirely new, the specific choice of modeling shaded and albedo channels *and* integrating it with an existing intrinsic decomposition model like IDArb shows insight. This strategy cleverly sidesteps the challenges of training a single model to generate all PBR channels directly, which is often plagued by data scarcity and domain gaps. However, the idea of combining generative models with material decomposition is becoming increasingly common.

    *   *Agentic Post-Processing:* The use of an MLLM for scoring and selection is a clever way to incorporate human-like artistic judgment into the automated texture generation process. This contributes to enhanced selection of results that are more aesthetically pleasing. However, the MLLM prompt engineering and selection process are not particularly unique, relying on existing capabilities of models like GPT-4. Furthermore, the benefit of MLLM scoring is somewhat limited by the underlying quality of the generated candidates from the diffusion model and decomposition.

*   **Significance:** The paper offers a practical approach to PBR texture generation, a challenging problem with significant applications in 3D content creation.

    *   *Improved Visual Quality:* The qualitative results showcase MuMA's ability to generate visually appealing and realistic textures, outperforming some existing methods. This is significant because high-quality textures are crucial for creating immersive and believable 3D experiences.

    *   *Potential Impact:* The paper could influence the development of more automated and user-friendly 3D content creation tools, enabling artists and designers to quickly generate high-quality textures for their models. However, the reliance on a strong intrinsic decomposition model and powerful LLMs (which can have compute costs and limitations) may restrict the widespread adoption of this method.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-motivated approach with clear explanations of the challenges in PBR texture generation.
    *   Clever integration of existing techniques (multi-view diffusion, material decomposition, MLLM scoring).
    *   Demonstrated improvements in visual quality compared to baselines.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation with qualitative and quantitative results, as well as user studies.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental novelty, building on existing techniques rather than presenting truly groundbreaking innovations.
    *   Dependence on existing powerful (and potentially resource-intensive) models (SDXL, IDArb, GPT-4). The performance is inherently limited by the capabilities of these underlying models.
    *   Limitations in handling complex geometries and imperfect training data, as discussed in the paper.
    *   The reported Ablation studies show that improvement in model quality comes primarily from the two new mechanisms introduced in the model, and little improvement comes from fine tuning the model.
