Experimental results demonstrate that Similar, through its step-wise, multi-dimensional assessment and synergistic gain, provides GVAs with effective intermediate signals during both training and inference-time scaling. The code is available at https://github.com/Galery23/Similar-v1.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces *Similar*, a novel step-wise, multi-dimensional, generalist reward model for boosting the learning and reasoning capabilities of Generalist Virtual Agents (GVAs) powered by Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  The key idea is to move away from purely outcome-based reward systems, which require labor-intensive human annotation, and towards a system that provides fine-grained feedback on each step of an agent's execution. The authors define five dimensions for evaluating agent actions: Helpfulness, Odds of Success, Efficiency, Task Relevance, and Coherence. They then develop an MCTS-P algorithm to automatically collect and annotate data based on these dimensions. They train *Similar* using a "Triple-M" (multi-step, multi-objective, multi-modal) strategy and introduce *SRM*, a new benchmark for evaluating reward models in the virtual agent domain. Experimental results demonstrate that *Similar* enhances GVA performance during both training and inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The paper presents a reasonably novel approach. The multi-dimensional reward structure is not entirely new (reward shaping and decomposition have been explored before), but the specific dimensions chosen, and the automated data collection through MCTS-P, do present a unique combination. The introduction of the *SRM* benchmark is also a valuable contribution. However, it's important to note that several components (MCTS, reward shaping, MLLMs) are well-established techniques, and the paper combines them in a specific configuration.

*Significance:*  The paper addresses a critical problem in GVA development: the bottleneck of human annotation for reward signal generation. By automating this process and providing more granular feedback, it has the potential to accelerate research in this area.  The *SRM* benchmark provides a standardized way to compare different reward models, which can help drive progress. The improvement in performance reported is substantial and demonstrates the practicality of the approach. However, the reliance on GPT-4 for some aspects of annotation is a potential limitation, as this model is not open-source or always readily available.

*Strengths:*
*   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing GVA training paradigms.
*   **Comprehensive solution:** *Similar* addresses these limitations through a well-designed framework.
*   **Automated data collection:** The MCTS-P algorithm reduces reliance on human annotation.
*   **Multi-dimensional assessment:** Captures diverse aspects of agent behavior.
*   **Benchmark dataset:** *SRM* provides a standardized evaluation platform.
*   **Experimental results:** Demonstrate significant performance improvements.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code.

*Weaknesses:*
*   **Dependency on GPT-4:**  Task Relevance and Coherence assessment relies on closed-source LLMs, potentially limiting reproducibility and accessibility. While the MCTS-P algorithm aims to automate data collection, GPT-4's involvement injects a dependency on a potentially unstable resource and raises concerns about biases in its assessments.
*   **Computational cost:** MCTS can be computationally expensive, which might limit scalability to more complex environments or tasks. The paper does not address the computational cost explicitly.
*   **Limited exploration of ethical considerations:** The impact statement briefly mentions ethical considerations, but doesn't delve into specifics concerning potential misuse of GVAs, or societal implications.
*   **Ablation study could be more thorough:** While the ablation study assesses the impact of individual dimensions, it would be beneficial to investigate interactions between dimensions in more detail, which could provide deeper insights.

*Potential Influence:* The paper has a good potential to influence the field of GVA research by:
*   Promoting the use of process-based reward models.
*   Encouraging the development of automated annotation techniques.
*   Providing a standardized benchmark for evaluating reward models.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing the limitations of outcome-based reward learning through step-wise, multi-dimensional evaluation. The MCTS-P algorithm automates the data collection process, offering a practical solution to the annotation bottleneck.  The proposed benchmark *SRM* will likely become a standard for evaluating reward models. While the approach combines existing techniques, the specific implementation and the resulting performance improvements are noteworthy.
However, the reliance on GPT-4 for some of the assessment introduces a dependency that impacts reproducibility and raises questions about biases. The limitations regarding computational cost and scalability of the proposed method are also present.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Human Motion Unlearning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18674v1)
- **Authors**: Edoardo De Matteis, Matteo Migliarini, Alessio Sampieri, Indro Spinelli, Fabio Galasso
- **Abstract**: We introduce the task of human motion unlearning to prevent the synthesis of toxic animations while preserving the general text-to-motion generative performance. Unlearning toxic motions is challenging as those can be generated from explicit text prompts and from implicit toxic combinations of safe motions (e.g., ``kicking" is ``loading and swinging a leg"). We propose the first motion unlearning benchmark by filtering toxic motions from the large and recent text-to-motion datasets of HumanML3D and Motion-X. We propose baselines, by adapting state-of-the-art image unlearning techniques to process spatio-temporal signals. Finally, we propose a novel motion unlearning model based on Latent Code Replacement, which we dub LCR. LCR is training-free and suitable to the discrete latent spaces of state-of-the-art text-to-motion diffusion models. LCR is simple and consistently outperforms baselines qualitatively and quantitatively. Project page: \href{https://www.pinlab.org/hmu}{https://www.pinlab.org/hmu}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the task of "human motion unlearning," aimed at preventing the generation of toxic or unethical animations by text-to-motion (T2M) models. It highlights that toxicity can arise not only from explicit text prompts but also from implicit combinations of safe motions. The authors create a benchmark by filtering toxic motions from the HumanML3D and Motion-X datasets. They propose baselines based on adapting state-of-the-art image unlearning techniques (UCE and RECE) to handle the spatio-temporal nature of motion. They introduce a novel training-free motion unlearning method called Latent Code Replacement (LCR), which operates in the discrete latent space of T2M diffusion models by replacing codebook entries associated with toxic motions. They demonstrate LCR's superior performance over baselines both qualitatively and quantitatively.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core contribution—introducing human motion unlearning as a problem and providing the first benchmark and dedicated approach (LCR)—is novel. The paper effectively identifies a gap in the T2M research, where ethical considerations related to generated content haven't been thoroughly addressed. While image unlearning has been explored, adapting and addressing the unique challenges of motion synthesis adds to the novelty. The LCR approach is also innovative, given the discrete latent space specificities of the T2M models used.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significant for several reasons:

    *   **Addressing a Real Problem:** The growing capabilities of T2M models raise ethical concerns. Preventing the generation of harmful content is crucial for responsible development and deployment of these technologies in applications like VR and animation.
    *   **Benchmarking Progress:** Establishing a benchmark allows for objective comparison and drives future research in motion unlearning.
    *   **Practical Approach:** The training-free nature of LCR makes it practical and easily adaptable to existing T2M models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear Problem Definition: The paper clearly articulates the problem of toxic motion generation.
    *   Solid Methodology: The proposed LCR method is well-motivated and effectively addresses the problem. The adaptations of UCE and RECE are logical.
    *   Comprehensive Evaluation: The experiments are thorough, with quantitative and qualitative comparisons against baselines on benchmark datasets.  The ablation studies provide insights into design choices.
    *   Practicality:  The emphasis on a training-free approach enhances practical applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on Keyword Filtering:  The benchmark creation relies on keyword filtering.  This is a good starting point, but keyword filters are inherently limited and can be circumvented.  A more sophisticated approach to identifying toxic motions in the future would strengthen the benchmark.
    *   Limited Generalization Analysis: The paper focuses primarily on a specific class of models (those using VQ-VAEs).  While this is justified, it would be good to discuss how the LCR method could be extended to other T2M architectures.
    *   Misuse Mitigation: The discussion about potential misuse is brief. Although mentioned it would be good to offer potential mitigation tactics.

* **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a positive impact by drawing attention to the ethical dimensions of T2M research and providing a practical solution for motion unlearning. It sets the stage for more research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper has substantial novelty in defining the problem of motion unlearning and proposing a dedicated solution. It is significant due to its ethical implications and the practical nature of the LCR method. While the keyword-based benchmark has limitations and the approach is currently tailored to specific model architectures, the overall contribution is solid and will likely stimulate further research. The benchmark provides a good starting point for further refinement and expansion by the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Commander-GPT: Fully Unleashing the Sarcasm Detection Capability of Multi-Modal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18681v1)
- **Authors**: Yazhou Zhang, Chunwang Zou, Bo Wang, Jing Qin
- **Abstract**: Sarcasm detection, as a crucial research direction in the field of Natural Language Processing (NLP), has attracted widespread attention. Traditional sarcasm detection tasks have typically focused on single-modal approaches (e.g., text), but due to the implicit and subtle nature of sarcasm, such methods often fail to yield satisfactory results. In recent years, researchers have shifted the focus of sarcasm detection to multi-modal approaches. However, effectively leveraging multi-modal information to accurately identify sarcastic content remains a challenge that warrants further exploration. Leveraging the powerful integrated processing capabilities of Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for various information sources, we propose an innovative multi-modal Commander-GPT framework. Inspired by military strategy, we first decompose the sarcasm detection task into six distinct sub-tasks. A central commander (decision-maker) then assigns the best-suited large language model to address each specific sub-task. Ultimately, the detection results from each model are aggregated to identify sarcasm. We conducted extensive experiments on MMSD and MMSD 2.0, utilizing four multi-modal large language models and six prompting strategies. Our experiments demonstrate that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance, with a 19.3% improvement in F1 score, without necessitating fine-tuning or ground-truth rationales.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Commander-GPT: Fully Unleashing the Sarcasm Detection Capability of Multi-Modal Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Commander-GPT, a novel framework designed to improve sarcasm detection in multi-modal settings using Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The core idea is to decompose the sarcasm detection task into several distinct sub-tasks (e.g., keyword extraction, sentiment analysis, facial expression recognition, image captioning, rhetorical device detection) and then assign these sub-tasks to specialized "general" models. A central "commander" model (like GPT-4) orchestrates the process, analyzing the input, assigning tasks, and integrating the results from each sub-model to make a final sarcasm determination.  The authors present experimental results on the MMSD and MMSD 2.0 datasets, demonstrating significant improvements over existing methods without requiring fine-tuning or ground-truth rationales.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The Commander-GPT framework presents a genuinely novel approach to multi-modal sarcasm detection. The task decomposition and orchestrated execution by specialized models are an interesting departure from monolithic models or simple feature fusion techniques. The "divide and conquer" strategy is well-motivated, especially considering the varying strengths and weaknesses of different models in different tasks.
    *   **Significant Performance Gains:** The reported performance improvements, especially the 19.3% F1 score gain, are substantial and compelling. This provides strong evidence that the Commander-GPT framework is effectively leveraging multi-modal information.
    *   **Practicality:** The framework avoids fine-tuning of models, making it more accessible and computationally efficient.  The emphasis on readily available models is a valuable aspect, allowing for wider adoption and experimentation.
    *   **Well-Defined Architecture:** The roles and responsibilities within the framework (commander, generals) are clearly defined, making the overall system easier to understand.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The paper includes ablation studies to demonstrate the impact of each 'general' model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Over-Reliance on GPT-4 as Commander:**  While using GPT-4 as a commander is a reasonable choice, it introduces a dependence on a proprietary model. The paper would be strengthened by including experiments using open-source alternatives as the commander, which would make it more accessible.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis Depth:** While the case study does provide some insight, a more in-depth analysis of the types of errors that Commander-GPT still makes would be beneficial. Quantifying the reduction in specific error types (e.g., those related to contextual misunderstanding) would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Lack of Generalizability Analysis:** While the authors tested the code on a limited scale for the SemEval 2018 task 3 dataset, a more thorough evaluation of the generalizability of Commander-GPT is needed. How does it perform on different datasets? Is the task decomposition strategy optimal for all multi-modal sarcasm detection scenarios?
    *   **Implicit Assumption of Task Independence:** The framework implicitly assumes that the sub-tasks are relatively independent. While task decomposition is useful, there might be inherent interdependencies. A more sophisticated approach that considers these interdependencies may further improve performance.
    *   **Limited ablation analysis:** It is unclear what specific types of models the ablation study included. It could include a model that is similar to the other generals used to evaluate the effectiveness of commander G.P.T.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of multi-modal sarcasm detection. The Commander-GPT framework provides a novel and effective way to leverage the strengths of different models.  The results are strong and demonstrate the potential of this approach.
    *   The task decomposition strategy could be applicable to other complex NLP problems beyond sarcasm detection.
    *   The focus on practical implementation and avoidance of fine-tuning is a valuable aspect for broader adoption.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a compelling and novel approach with strong performance results, it has some limitations that prevent it from achieving a higher score. The dependence on GPT-4, the relatively limited error analysis, and the lack of a deep investigation of generalizability somewhat reduce its impact. The assumptions about sub-task independence also warrant further exploration. The ablation studies could also be further clarified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLaVAction: evaluating and training multi-modal large language models for action recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18712v1)
- **Authors**: Shaokai Ye, Haozhe Qi, Alexander Mathis, Mackenzie W. Mathis
- **Abstract**: Understanding human behavior requires measuring behavioral actions. Due to its complexity, behavior is best mapped onto a rich, semantic structure such as language. The recent development of multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) is a promising candidate for a wide range of action understanding tasks. In this work, we focus on evaluating and then improving MLLMs to perform action recognition. We reformulate EPIC-KITCHENS-100, one of the largest and most challenging egocentric action datasets, to the form of video multiple question answering (EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA). We show that when we sample difficult incorrect answers as distractors, leading MLLMs struggle to recognize the correct actions. We propose a series of methods that greatly improve the MLLMs' ability to perform action recognition, achieving state-of-the-art on both the EPIC-KITCHENS-100 validation set, as well as outperforming GPT-4o by 21 points in accuracy on EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA. Lastly, we show improvements on other action-related video benchmarks such as EgoSchema, PerceptionTest, LongVideoBench, VideoMME and MVBench, suggesting that MLLMs are a promising path forward for complex action tasks. Code and models are available at: https://github.com/AdaptiveMotorControlLab/LLaVAction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLaVAction, a method for improving the performance of Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in action recognition tasks.  The authors reformulate the EPIC-KITCHENS-100 dataset into a video Multiple Choice Question & Answer (MQA) format called EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA. A key aspect is the use of a state-of-the-art action recognition model (TIM) to generate hard negative distractors for the MQA task, making it more challenging for MLLMs. They then propose several techniques including vision token supervision, temporal detection, and memory of prior actions to improve MLLM performance.  The resulting LLaVAction model achieves state-of-the-art results on the EPIC-KITCHENS-100 validation set and the proposed EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA benchmark, even outperforming GPT-4o. They demonstrate improvements on other action-related benchmarks as well.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has a few novel contributions. The reformulation of EPIC-KITCHENS-100 into a more challenging MQA format using hard negative sampling via a strong action recognition model is a solid contribution. The proposed training techniques (vision token supervision, temporal detection, prior action memory) are not individually groundbreaking, but their combination and demonstrated effectiveness is a valuable addition. The overall system design and the performance improvements are notable.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a real weakness of existing MLLMs: difficulty in action recognition, especially when visually similar options are present. This work highlights a need to improve the visual performance of MLLMs and to benchmark them in such a realistic scenario that is challenging because of similar actions that can happen at random. Successfully improving action recognition with these types of methods is significant for applications like robotics and augmented reality. Demonstrating that these improvements transfer to other related video benchmarks reinforces its potential influence.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The creation of EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA offers a more robust and challenging benchmark than existing datasets, addressing the limitations of fixed vocabularies and unclear distractor difficulty.
    *   The performance gains on both EPIC-KITCHENS-100 and EPIC-KITCHENS-100-MQA are substantial, especially the outperformance of GPT-4o.
    *   The authors provide a detailed ablation study, which elucidates the contribution of each proposed component.
    *   The demonstration of improvement on other video benchmarks is promising.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the proposed techniques are effective, the individual components (vision token supervision, temporal detection etc.) are not radically new ideas. The innovation lies more in the specific application and orchestration.
    *   The reliance on a specific action recognition model (TIM) for hard negative generation raises questions about generalization to other scenarios where such a model may not be available or as effective.
    *   Some descriptions in the paper are not explicit. For example, the details of the hyperparameters are buried at the end of a paragraph. This limits the ability to reproduce.

*   **Impact:**  This paper has a solid potential impact.  The newly formulated MQA benchmark is of use to the research community to better evaluate future MLLMs. The demonstrated improvements can influence future model designs, especially in integrating better visual understanding components into MLLMs.  The comprehensive evaluation across multiple benchmarks will encourage further research in this direction.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign this paper a score of 7. The work addresses a recognized problem in MLLMs with a novel approach to benchmark creation. The performance improvements are significant, and the evaluation is thorough. However, the individual components of the method are not fundamentally new. The reliance on specific dataset characteristics and a pretrained model (TIM) for hard negative generation limits its generalizability to some extent. However, the results are compelling and can be of immediate benefit to the community.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### GS-Marker: Generalizable and Robust Watermarking for 3D Gaussian Splatting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18718v1)
- **Authors**: Lijiang Li, Jinglu Wang, Xiang Ming, Yan Lu
- **Abstract**: In the Generative AI era, safeguarding 3D models has become increasingly urgent. While invisible watermarking is well-established for 2D images with encoder-decoder frameworks, generalizable and robust solutions for 3D remain elusive. The main difficulty arises from the renderer between the 3D encoder and 2D decoder, which disrupts direct gradient flow and complicates training. Existing 3D methods typically rely on per-scene iterative optimization, resulting in time inefficiency and limited generalization. In this work, we propose a single-pass watermarking approach for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), a well-known yet underexplored representation for watermarking. We identify two major challenges: (1) ensuring effective training generalized across diverse 3D models, and (2) reliably extracting watermarks from free-view renderings, even under distortions. Our framework, named GS-Marker, incorporates a 3D encoder to embed messages, distortion layers to enhance resilience against various distortions, and a 2D decoder to extract watermarks from renderings. A key innovation is the Adaptive Marker Control mechanism that adaptively perturbs the initially optimized 3DGS, escaping local minima and improving both training stability and convergence. Extensive experiments show that GS-Marker outperforms per-scene training approaches in terms of decoding accuracy and model fidelity, while also significantly reducing computation time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GS-Marker, a novel framework for robust and generalizable watermarking of 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) models.  Unlike existing methods that rely on per-scene iterative optimization, GS-Marker employs a single-pass watermarking approach. The framework consists of a 3D encoder that embeds messages, distortion layers to enhance resilience, and a 2D decoder to extract watermarks from renderings.  A key contribution is the Adaptive Marker Control (AMC) mechanism, which adaptively perturbs the 3DGS to escape local minima during training, improving stability and convergence. Experiments demonstrate that GS-Marker outperforms per-scene training methods in terms of decoding accuracy, model fidelity, and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a significant gap in the area of 3D watermarking. While watermarking for 2D images is well-established, generalizable and efficient methods for 3D models, particularly 3DGS, are limited. The single-pass watermarking approach is a clear improvement over existing iterative methods, increasing efficiency and generalizability. The Adaptive Marker Control (AMC) mechanism is also a novel contribution, demonstrating a solution to the challenge of incorporating watermarks into already optimized 3DGS models without significantly degrading rendering quality.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical impact on protecting intellectual property in the rapidly growing field of AI-generated 3D assets. The method's generalizability (demonstrated across different datasets) and robustness (tested under various distortions) make it a potentially valuable tool for content creators and platforms. The reduction in computation time compared to iterative optimization methods is also a substantial advantage, making it more scalable and applicable to real-world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficiency:** The single-pass watermarking approach significantly reduces the computational cost compared to iterative optimization methods.
    *   **Generalizability:** Demonstrated across multiple 3DGS datasets without fine-tuning.
    *   **Robustness:**  Designed to withstand various distortions, improving watermark extraction from rendered images.
    *   **Novelty:** The Adaptive Marker Control (AMC) is a novel and effective solution for balancing watermark embedding and rendering fidelity.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper clearly explains the challenges of 3D watermarking and presents the proposed solution in a logical and understandable manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Embedding Capacity:** The paper mentions that each Gaussian primitive integrates message bits, potentially limiting embedding capacity.  Future work needs to improve embedding capacity.
    *   **Quantitative advantage over state-of-the-art techniques:** As a novel work, GS-Marker demonstrates reasonable performance improvement over optimization-based methods. Further studies should focus on benchmarking against additional generalizable watermarking techniques.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in 3D watermarking, particularly for 3DGS models. The single-pass approach and the Adaptive Marker Control mechanism could serve as building blocks for more advanced and efficient watermarking techniques. The paper may also encourage the development of standardized benchmarks and evaluation metrics for 3D watermarking methods.

*   **Justification of Score:**  The paper offers a substantial advancement in 3DGS watermarking, addressing significant challenges and demonstrating practical benefits. While there is room for improvement in embedding capacity and more benchmarks against other generalizable watermarking strategies, the proposed framework is a significant step forward in this domain.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Boosting Resolution Generalization of Diffusion Transformers with Randomized Positional Encodings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18719v1)
- **Authors**: Cong Liu, Liang Hou, Mingwu Zheng, Xin Tao, Pengfei Wan, Di Zhang, Kun Gai
- **Abstract**: Resolution generalization in image generation tasks enables the production of higher-resolution images with lower training resolution overhead. However, a significant challenge in resolution generalization, particularly in the widely used Diffusion Transformers, lies in the mismatch between the positional encodings encountered during testing and those used during training. While existing methods have employed techniques such as interpolation, extrapolation, or their combinations, none have fully resolved this issue. In this paper, we propose a novel two-dimensional randomized positional encodings (RPE-2D) framework that focuses on learning positional order of image patches instead of the specific distances between them, enabling seamless high- and low-resolution image generation without requiring high- and low-resolution image training. Specifically, RPE-2D independently selects positions over a broader range along both the horizontal and vertical axes, ensuring that all position encodings are trained during the inference phase, thus improving resolution generalization. Additionally, we propose a random data augmentation technique to enhance the modeling of position order. To address the issue of image cropping caused by the augmentation, we introduce corresponding micro-conditioning to enable the model to perceive the specific cropping patterns. On the ImageNet dataset, our proposed RPE-2D achieves state-of-the-art resolution generalization performance, outperforming existing competitive methods when trained at a resolution of $256 \times 256$ and inferred at $384 \times 384$ and $512 \times 512$, as well as when scaling from $512 \times 512$ to $768 \times 768$ and $1024 \times 1024$. And it also exhibits outstanding capabilities in low-resolution image generation, multi-stage training acceleration and multi-resolution inheritance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Randomized Positional Encodings (RPE-2D), a novel approach to improve resolution generalization in diffusion transformer models (DiTs) for image generation.  RPE-2D aims to address the mismatch between positional encodings used during training and testing, a key limitation when generating higher-resolution images with models trained at lower resolutions. The core idea is to learn the *order* of image patches rather than the specific distances between them, enabling seamless high- and low-resolution image generation without retraining.  RPE-2D employs independent random sampling of positions along horizontal and vertical axes during training, combined with data augmentation and micro-conditioning to improve the model's understanding of the spatial layout.  The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art resolution generalization performance on ImageNet, outperforming existing methods when trained at 256x256 and inferred at higher resolutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in reframing the resolution generalization problem in diffusion transformers as an issue of learning positional order rather than absolute distances.  While randomized positional encodings have been used in language models (LLMs), their adaptation and application to 2D image data, specifically within the DiT framework, is novel. The development of RPE-2D with horizontal and vertical axis decoupling, along with data augmentation and micro-conditioning to preserve positional order in 2D images is also an original contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical limitation of diffusion models – the high computational cost of training at high resolutions. By significantly improving resolution generalization, RPE-2D reduces training overhead, making high-resolution image generation more accessible. The results on ImageNet demonstrate a substantial improvement over existing methods, suggesting that RPE-2D could become a valuable technique in the field.  The idea of focusing on positional order rather than precise distance also opens avenues for other applications where spatial relationships are important. The potential benefits of low-resolution image generation and multi-stage acceleration are worth exploring. The paper also demonstrates that the approach is compatible with several positional encoding techniques such as SinPE and RoPE.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenge of resolution generalization in diffusion transformers.
    *   **Novel Approach:** RPE-2D provides a novel perspective and a technically sound solution to the problem.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on ImageNet convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of RPE-2D, outperforming existing methods.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.
    *   **Practical Impact:** RPE-2D can significantly reduce the training costs associated with high-resolution image generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses specifically on resolution generalization in DiTs and does not explore applications to other generative models or computer vision tasks, beyond those mentioned in the conclusion.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study, while useful, could be more detailed.  A more comprehensive evaluation of different design choices within RPE-2D would strengthen the paper. More discussion on the impact of changing values of maximum positions H and W could be added.
    *   **Explanation of Order Preservation:** While the paper discusses the importance of preserving positional order, the exact mechanisms and benefits could be explained more thoroughly. The contribution of a low-rank order approach may also be beneficial to explore in more depth.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's impact could be substantial. The idea of decoupling positional order from absolute distance and using randomized encodings could inspire new research directions in generative modeling and other computer vision tasks. The technique is easily implementable and could quickly become a standard practice for training DiTs when high-resolution image generation is desired with limited computational resources.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-validated technique that addresses a key limitation of diffusion transformer models. The performance gains are significant, and the approach has the potential to reduce training costs and improve the accessibility of high-resolution image generation. While the scope is somewhat limited and there are minor areas for improvement, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Thermalizer: Stable autoregressive neural emulation of spatiotemporal chaos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18731v1)
- **Authors**: Chris Pedersen, Laure Zanna, Joan Bruna
- **Abstract**: Autoregressive surrogate models (or \textit{emulators}) of spatiotemporal systems provide an avenue for fast, approximate predictions, with broad applications across science and engineering. At inference time, however, these models are generally unable to provide predictions over long time rollouts due to accumulation of errors leading to diverging trajectories. In essence, emulators operate out of distribution, and controlling the online distribution quickly becomes intractable in large-scale settings. To address this fundamental issue, and focusing on time-stationary systems admitting an invariant measure, we leverage diffusion models to obtain an implicit estimator of the score of this invariant measure. We show that this model of the score function can be used to stabilize autoregressive emulator rollouts by applying on-the-fly denoising during inference, a process we call \textit{thermalization}. Thermalizing an emulator rollout is shown to extend the time horizon of stable predictions by an order of magnitude in complex systems exhibiting turbulent and chaotic behavior, opening up a novel application of diffusion models in the context of neural emulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Thermalizer: Stable autoregressive neural emulation of spatiotemporal chaos":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "thermalization" method to improve the long-term stability of autoregressive neural emulators for spatiotemporal chaotic systems. The core idea leverages diffusion models trained on the stationary distribution of the system to "denoise" or "thermalize" emulator rollouts at inference time. This effectively prevents the emulator from drifting out-of-distribution due to error accumulation, significantly extending the time horizon over which stable and accurate predictions can be made. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of this technique on two challenging high-dimensional turbulent systems: Kolmogorov flow and a quasi-geostrophic (QG) turbulent system. They show that thermalization allows for stable rollouts over orders of magnitude longer timescales compared to standard autoregressive emulators.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of using diffusion models to stabilize autoregressive emulator rollouts is genuinely novel. Prior work has focused on architectural modifications, training data augmentation, or incorporating information about system invariants during training. The approach of decoupling the emulator and stabilizer (diffusion model), training them independently, and combining them at inference time provides a new perspective. The approach of using the *reverse* diffusion to correct errors is also a clever idea, which distinguishes it from pure generative uses of diffusion models.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a major bottleneck in applying neural emulators to complex dynamical systems: long-term instability. Overcoming this limitation opens up possibilities for using neural emulators in a wider range of applications, such as climate modeling and weather forecasting, where long-term simulations are essential. Furthermore, the modularity of the approach is significant; it means existing emulators can be stabilized without retraining, and pre-trained diffusion models can be readily applied. This could be a major boon to the research community by providing an orthogonal approach to existing model architectures and training strategies. The ability to perform arbitrarily long rollouts from a computationally inexpensive emulator is also a major breakthrough with tremendous practical utility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The "thermalization" concept is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The use of diffusion models is theoretically justified.
    *   The modular design allows for easy integration with existing emulators.
    *   The experimental results on Kolmogorov flow and QG turbulence convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The experiments analyzing the number of thermalization steps, MSE relative to truth, and autocorrelation provide insightful data demonstrating the value of the method.
    *   The thorough exploration of hyperparameters for both the emulator architecture and the diffusion model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The Gaussianity assumption on perturbations is a significant limitation and could hinder applicability to certain systems. While the experiments show robustness, a more detailed analysis of the impact of non-Gaussian errors would be valuable.
    *   The approach relies on careful tuning of hyperparameters (Sinit, Sstop), which may require significant effort for new systems. More adaptive or automated methods for setting these parameters would increase the practicality of the method.
    *   The current framework is limited to time-homogeneous systems. Extending it to autonomous systems with periodic forcing is an important direction for future work. The current work relies on learning a stationary data distribution with diffusion, so temporal periodicity might require more sophisticated diffusion model architectures.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of neural emulation for dynamical systems. The thermalization method provides a practical and effective way to address long-term instability, enabling the use of neural emulators in a broader range of applications.  The paper's modularity should encourage other researchers to explore this approach and build upon it.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and technically sound method that addresses a critical limitation of neural emulators for spatiotemporal chaotic systems. The experimental results are convincing, and the approach is relatively easy to implement. While the Gaussianity assumption and hyperparameter sensitivity are limitations, the potential impact of the work is substantial, making it a strong contribution to the field. The method provides an orthogonal approach to other work on model architecture and training, and provides an immediate advantage to the user community.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Mechanistic Interpretability of Fine-Tuned Vision Transformers on Distorted Images: Decoding Attention Head Behavior for Transparent and Trustworthy AI
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18762v1)
- **Authors**: Nooshin Bahador
- **Abstract**: Mechanistic interpretability improves the safety, reliability, and robustness of large AI models. This study examined individual attention heads in vision transformers (ViTs) fine tuned on distorted 2D spectrogram images containing non relevant content (axis labels, titles, color bars). By introducing extraneous features, the study analyzed how transformer components processed unrelated information, using mechanistic interpretability to debug issues and reveal insights into transformer architectures. Attention maps assessed head contributions across layers. Heads in early layers (1 to 3) showed minimal task impact with ablation increased MSE loss slightly ({\mu}=0.11%, {\sigma}=0.09%), indicating focus on less critical low level features. In contrast, deeper heads (e.g., layer 6) caused a threefold higher loss increase ({\mu}=0.34%, {\sigma}=0.02%), demonstrating greater task importance. Intermediate layers (6 to 11) exhibited monosemantic behavior, attending exclusively to chirp regions. Some early heads (1 to 4) were monosemantic but non task relevant (e.g. text detectors, edge or corner detectors). Attention maps distinguished monosemantic heads (precise chirp localization) from polysemantic heads (multiple irrelevant regions). These findings revealed functional specialization in ViTs, showing how heads processed relevant vs. extraneous information. By decomposing transformers into interpretable components, this work enhanced model understanding, identified vulnerabilities, and advanced safer, more transparent AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the mechanistic interpretability of Vision Transformers (ViTs) fine-tuned on distorted spectrogram images. The study analyzes how individual attention heads process relevant and irrelevant information (axis labels, titles, color bars). Through ablation studies, the paper identifies the functional specialization of heads across layers, with early layers focusing on low-level features, intermediate layers attending exclusively to chirp regions (monosemantic behavior), and some heads acting as non-task-relevant feature detectors (e.g., text or edge detectors). The research distinguishes between monosemantic heads (precise chirp localization) and polysemantic heads (multiple irrelevant regions), contributing to a better understanding of ViTs and potentially leading to safer and more transparent AI. The methodology involves extracting attention maps, performing ablation analysis by zeroing out weights, and evaluating the resulting impact on MSE loss.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of mechanistic interpretability techniques to Vision Transformers trained on spectrograms with distortions, a specific domain not as widely explored as natural images. Specifically, the identification of monosemantic vs. polysemantic heads, and their corresponding functionality related or unrelated to the main task, presents an important new insight. Disentangling how ViTs handle extraneous information is relevant for robustness.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing importance of trustworthy AI. The ability to dissect ViTs and understand how they process information, especially irrelevant data, is crucial for ensuring robustness and reliability. Debugging issues and finding ways to train more efficiently by removing unnecessary parameters from early layers and focusing on the relevant heads helps in the development of AI models that are easier to understand and debug, and it offers ways to improve model robustness. However, this is mainly demonstrated on a limited dataset.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear methodology: The paper describes the methodology in detail, which includes attention map extraction, ablation analysis, and MSE loss evaluation.
    *   Insightful findings: Identification of functional specialization across layers and the distinction between monosemantic and polysemantic heads.
    *   Relevance: The work addresses the key area of trustworthy AI by focusing on understanding the inner workings of complex models.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Scope: The study is confined to a specific dataset (synthetic spectrograms). This limits the generalizability of the findings to other domains and types of images.
    *   Quantitative Results: While the ablation study provides quantitative data (MSE loss), more detailed analysis of the impact of ablating specific heads could have been provided. It would be worthwhile, for example, to compare the variance in the loss between layers/heads in greater detail.
    *   Limited Application: The immediate practical application of the findings is not fully explored. While the paper points to potential optimization strategies, it does not demonstrate these optimizations in practice.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in mechanistic interpretability, particularly in the context of spectral analysis and ViTs. Future work could build on these findings to develop more robust and efficient ViT architectures.
*   **Justification:** The paper contributes a valuable case study on how to apply mechanistic interpretability to a specific domain. However, it is somewhat limited in its generalizability. The core contribution lies in the applied methodology to understand functional separation of heads and their contribution to the task. Also there is not a lot of discussion on other papers in the domain; hence, the novelty looks high. Also, the ablation experiments lack some rigorous statistical analysis.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### AlphaSpace: Enabling Robotic Actions through Semantic Tokenization and Symbolic Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18769v1)
- **Authors**: Alan Dao, Dinh Bach Vu, Bui Quang Huy
- **Abstract**: This paper presents AlphaSpace, a novel methodology designed to enhance the spatial reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) for 3D Cartesian space navigation. AlphaSpace employs a semantics-based tokenization strategy, encoding height information through specialized semantic tokens, and integrates primarily symbolic synthetic reasoning data. This approach enables LLMs to accurately manipulate objects by positioning them at specific [x, y, z] coordinates. Experimental results demonstrate that AlphaSpace significantly outperforms existing models on manipulation subtasks, achieving a total accuracy of 66.67%, compared to 37.5% for GPT-4o and 29.17% for Claude 3.5 Sonnet.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "AlphaSpace: Enabling Robotic Actions through Semantic Tokenization and Symbolic Reasoning" presents a new methodology, AlphaSpace, designed to improve the spatial reasoning abilities of language models (LLMs) for robotic manipulation in 3D Cartesian space. AlphaSpace utilizes a hierarchical semantics-based tokenization strategy to encode spatial information at both coarse and fine-grained levels, representing objects with their attributes, positions, and height information through structured tokens. This approach aims to enable precise spatial reasoning without relying on traditional vision-based embeddings. The paper describes the synthetic data generation pipeline, model training process, and experimental results on the EmbodiedBench benchmark. The results demonstrate that AlphaSpace significantly outperforms existing models (GPT-40 and Claude 3.5 Sonnet) on manipulation subtasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Approach:** The key strength of the paper lies in its attempt to move away from heavy reliance on visual embeddings in robotics. The semantic tokenization approach for representing spatial information is innovative and could offer a more efficient and interpretable alternative.
    *   **Clarity and Structure:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology, including the data generation process and model training pipeline.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on EmbodiedBench provide empirical evidence to support the claims. The results demonstrate a significant performance improvement over strong baselines, highlighting the potential of the proposed approach.
    *   **Addressing a Key Challenge:** The paper directly addresses the challenge of spatial reasoning in 3D environments, which is a crucial aspect of robotic manipulation.
    *   The paper identifies an open problem and attempts to provide a solution with interesting ideas.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Synthetic Data Dependency:** A major limitation is the reliance on synthetic data. The transferability of the learned representations to real-world scenarios, where visual input is noisy and unstructured, is questionable. The gap between the synthetic environment and real-world complexity is significant.
    *   **Limited Task Scope:** The experiments are confined to relatively simple tabletop manipulation tasks. The scalability of AlphaSpace to more complex robotic scenarios, such as multi-step assembly or dynamic obstacle avoidance, is not demonstrated.
    *   **Limited Baselines:** While the paper compares against GPT-40 and Claude 3.5 Sonnet, the baselines aren't directly tailored to spatial reasoning or robotic manipulation. Comparing to other models specifically designed for this type of task would strengthen the results.  The absence of comparisons against other VLA or foundation models that handle spatial reasoning weakens the claims of state-of-the-art performance. The models are not actually state-of-the-art.
    *   **Overstated Claims:** The paper claims to significantly outperform state-of-the-art, but given the very specific setting and limited comparative benchmarks it seems a bit too optimistic.

*   **Novelty:** The use of semantic tokenization to represent spatial information for robotic manipulation is reasonably novel, particularly in its explicit focus on 3D reasoning without vision reliance. This approach is a notable departure from the standard VLM paradigm. However, the fundamental concept of spatial encoding isn't entirely new, and the degree of innovation is moderate rather than revolutionary.

*   **Significance:** If the approach can be extended to real-world scenarios, it could have a meaningful impact on the field. The potential for efficiency and interpretability is appealing. However, until the robustness of AlphaSpace is proven in more complex, real-world settings, its significance remains somewhat limited.
*   **Synthetic Dataset:** The heavy reliance on synthetic data, while beneficial for training, poses challenges when translating to real-world applications. This approach might not fully capture the complexities of natural environments and could lead to limitations in the model's generalizability.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6**.
Here's the rationale:

*   The paper introduces a moderately novel approach to spatial reasoning in robotics, but its practical impact is unclear due to its reliance on synthetic data and limited task scope.
*   The empirical results are encouraging, but the lack of comparison against more relevant baselines diminishes the impact of the findings.
*   While the paper identifies an important challenge, it provides only a partial solution that requires further development before it can be considered truly significant. The approach needs to be robustified in real-world scenarios before its true potential can be realized.
*   The paper's contribution is valuable in presenting an alternative paradigm for spatial reasoning, but the experimental scope does not fully justify the claims.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### BitDecoding: Unlocking Tensor Cores for Long-Context LLMs Decoding with Low-Bit KV Cache
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18773v1)
- **Authors**: Dayou Du, Shijie Cao, Jianyi Cheng, Ting Cao, Mao Yang
- **Abstract**: The growing adoption of long-context Large Language Models (LLMs) has introduced significant memory and computational challenges in autoregressive decoding due to the expanding Key-Value (KV) cache. KV cache quantization has emerged as a promising solution, with prior work showing that 4-bit or even 2-bit quantization can maintain model accuracy while reducing memory costs. However, despite these benefits, preliminary implementations for the low-bit KV cache struggle to deliver the expected speedup due to quantization and dequantization overheads and the lack of Tensor Cores utilization. In this work, we propose BitDecoding, a GPU-optimized framework that unlocks Tensor Cores for efficient decoding with low-bit KV cache. Efficiently leveraging Tensor Cores for low-bit KV cache is challenging due to the dynamic nature of KV cache generation at each decoding step. BitDecoding addresses these challenges with a Tensor Cores-Centric BitFusion Scheme that ensures data layout compatibility to enable high utilization of Tensor Cores. Additionally, BitDecoding incorporates a warp-efficient parallel decoding kernel and a fine-grained asynchronous pipeline, minimizing dequantization overhead and improving computational efficiency. Experiments show that BitDecoding achieves up to 7.5x speedup on RTX 4090, 4.8x on A100, and 8.9x on H100, compared to FP16 FlashDecoding-v2. It also outperforms the state-of-the-art low-bit KV cache implementation (QServe) by up to 4.3x. On LLaMA-3.1-8B with a 128K sequence length, BitDecoding reduces single-batch decoding latency by 3x, demonstrating its effectiveness in long-context generation scenarios. The code is available at https://github.com/DD-DuDa/BitDecoding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "BitDecoding: Unlocking Tensor Cores for Long-Context LLMs Decoding with Low-Bit KV Cache" proposes a novel GPU-optimized framework for accelerating the autoregressive decoding of long-context Large Language Models (LLMs) using low-bit Key-Value (KV) cache quantization. The core innovation lies in efficiently leveraging Tensor Cores for mixed-precision matrix multiplications while minimizing the overhead associated with quantization and dequantization. BitDecoding introduces a "Tensor Cores-Centric BitFusion Scheme" to ensure data layout compatibility, a "Warps-Efficient Parallel Dequantization" technique to reduce dequantization overhead, and an asynchronous pipeline for seamless cooperation between CUDA cores and Tensor Cores.  Experimental results demonstrate significant speedups compared to FP16 FlashDecoding-v2 and other low-bit KV cache implementations like QServe, particularly in long-context scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Addresses a critical problem:** The paper tackles a relevant and increasingly important challenge in LLM deployment: the memory and computational bottleneck caused by the expanding KV cache during long-context decoding.
    * **Novelty:** The paper provides a genuine contribution to the field.  While KV cache quantization is not new, the specific approach of tightly integrating it with Tensor Core utilization, specifically addressing the layout mismatches and dequantization overheads, appears to be novel.  The "Tensor Cores-Centric BitFusion Scheme" and "Warps-Efficient Parallel Dequantization" are key innovations.  The system-level design is another strong point.
    * **Comprehensive evaluation:** The evaluation is thorough, covering kernel-level benchmarks and end-to-end model inference on different GPU architectures (RTX 4090, A100, H100).  The comparisons against strong baselines like FlashDecoding-v2, KIVI, Atom, and QServe strengthens the results.  The ablation study provides insights into the effectiveness of individual components.
    * **Significant performance improvements:** The performance gains are substantial, with reported speedups of up to 7.5x compared to FP16 FlashDecoding-v2. A 3x speedup on LLaMA-3.1-8B with a 128K sequence length is impressive and demonstrates practical benefits.
    * **Open-source code:** Making the code publicly available enhances reproducibility and facilitates adoption by the community.
    * **Leveraging Hopper Asynchronous Execution:** Exploiting the newer Hopper GPU features for asynchronous execution demonstrates an understanding of modern GPU architectures and maximizes hardware utilization.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The system design, while effective, appears complex with multiple components (Residual Kernel, Packing Kernel, Combined Kernel), layout transformations, and synchronization mechanisms. This complexity might pose a barrier to entry for some researchers or practitioners.  A clearer, more simplified presentation of the system architecture would be beneficial.
    * **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on optimizing the decoding phase.  While important, future work could explore extending the techniques to the prefill phase. Also, the focus is on NVIDIA GPUs; exploring applicability to other hardware platforms (e.g., AMD GPUs, TPUs) would broaden the impact.
    * **Limited Accuracy Evaluation:** While the paper reports accuracy on LongBench, a more detailed accuracy evaluation on various long-context tasks would be desirable, especially given the use of quantization. Specifically, tasks sensitive to long-range dependencies could show the effectiveness (or limitations) of this approach.
    * **Reliance on Specific Architectures:**  While leveraging newer architectures is good, there should be discussion of the potential portability limitations when older GPUs do not include the specific features they are using.
    * **Discussion of Quantization Tradeoffs:** A deeper discussion regarding specific quantization methods is needed and how they will impact latency and accuracy.

* **Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM deployment by enabling efficient and scalable inference of long-context models. The techniques developed can contribute to reducing the memory footprint and improving the throughput of LLM serving systems, making it more feasible to deploy LLMs with large context windows on resource-constrained devices.  The tight integration of quantization with Tensor Core utilization provides a blueprint for future research in this area.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper demonstrates a combination of novelty, technical depth, and practical impact. It provides a well-engineered solution to a pressing problem in LLM deployment.  The thorough evaluation, strong performance results, and open-source code contribute to its significance. The identified weaknesses, such as the system's complexity and the lack of more thorough investigation into accuracy, are outweighed by the strengths. A score of 8 acknowledges the paper's important contribution while also recognizing the areas for future improvement.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.AR
- **Score**: 8/10

### REALM: A Dataset of Real-World LLM Use Cases
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18792v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Cheng, Kshitish Ghate, Wenyue Hua, William Yang Wang, Hong Shen, Fei Fang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models, such as the GPT series, have driven significant industrial applications, leading to economic and societal transformations. However, a comprehensive understanding of their real-world applications remains limited. To address this, we introduce REALM, a dataset of over 94,000 LLM use cases collected from Reddit and news articles. REALM captures two key dimensions: the diverse applications of LLMs and the demographics of their users. It categorizes LLM applications and explores how users' occupations relate to the types of applications they use. By integrating real-world data, REALM offers insights into LLM adoption across different domains, providing a foundation for future research on their evolving societal roles. A dedicated dashboard https://realm-e7682.web.app/ presents the data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "REALM: A Dataset of Real-World LLM Use Cases":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces REALM, a new dataset of over 94,000 LLM use cases collected from Reddit and news articles between June 2020 and December 2024.  The dataset aims to provide a comprehensive understanding of how LLMs are being deployed and used in real-world scenarios.  REALM focuses on two primary dimensions: the specific applications of LLMs (categorized using a comprehensive taxonomy) and the occupations of the users (categorized using the O*NET database). The authors detail their data collection and annotation pipeline, including keyword filtering and a four-stage annotation process utilizing GPT-4o-mini. They also provide initial statistical analyses of the dataset, demonstrating potential insights into trends, policy implications, and cross-domain research. A dedicated interactive dashboard and API are provided for data exploration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation and release of a large-scale, curated dataset specifically focused on real-world LLM use cases and their connection to user occupations. While previous work has explored LLM impact using occupation-activity typologies or social media data, REALM attempts to bridge the gap by providing a dataset based on both news articles *and* Reddit discussions, categorized using a robust taxonomy and occupation classification. This integrated approach is a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of REALM stems from its potential to inform various areas of research and development related to LLMs.

    *   **Social Science/HCI:**  Provides a more grounded empirical basis for understanding the societal impact of LLMs, moving beyond expert opinions or predefined rubrics.
    *   **Policy Making:**  Data on real-world usage can inform policies related to AI adoption, workforce development, and ethical considerations.
    *   **LLM Development:**  Insights into how LLMs are being used can help developers better align their models with real-world needs and address emerging challenges.
    *   **Bias and Fairness Research:** The dataset offers a valuable resource for investigating biases that emerge from user-generated content in LLM applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Size and Scope:**  The sheer size of the dataset (94,000+ instances) provides a solid foundation for statistical analysis.
    *   **Comprehensive Taxonomy:**  Using both LLM application and occupation taxonomies allows for multifaceted analysis. The paper highlights the effort in refining these taxonomies to fit the dataset.
    *   **Data Collection Methodology:**  The combination of news articles and Reddit posts offers a more balanced view than either source alone.  The filtering and annotation pipeline appears to be well-designed to ensure data quality and relevance.
    *   **Accessibility:**  The interactive dashboard and API make the dataset accessible for a wide range of researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology and initial findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Source Bias:** The dataset is inherently biased by the sources used (Reddit and news articles). As acknowledged by the authors, this leads to an overrepresentation of certain fields (Computer Science, Business) and underrepresentation of others. While the authors address this, it remains a limitation.
    *   **Annotation Reliance on LLMs:**  While GPT-4o-mini is used for annotation, this introduces the potential for biases inherent in the LLM itself. The paper addresses this through multiple quality control steps and expert review, but this is still a concern.
    *   **Limited Analysis:** The paper primarily presents descriptive statistics. Deeper analysis, such as identifying trends over time or exploring relationships between LLM use cases and specific occupations, would further enhance the paper's value.
    *   **Generalizability of Reddit Data:** The degree to which Reddit conversations represents the views and behaviors of the broader population may also be debated.
    *   **Lack of Details on the "Multi-Label Linking Module":** While the four-stage pipeline is described, the description of the Multi-Label Linking Module lacks the detail provided for the other three modules.

*   **Potential Influence:** The dataset has the potential to become a widely used resource in the LLM research community. Its accessible format and comprehensive scope make it attractive for various research projects. The initial analysis presented in the paper already provides valuable insights, and further research using REALM is likely to yield additional findings.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the points above, the paper represents a valuable, but not groundbreaking, contribution. The dataset is a significant resource and addresses an important gap in understanding real-world LLM usage. The limitations related to source bias and the reliance on GPT-4o-mini for annotation are acknowledged but still impact the strength of the work. While the initial analysis is promising, the full potential of the dataset remains to be explored.

### Classical Planning with LLM-Generated Heuristics: Challenging the State of the Art with Python Code
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18809v1)
- **Authors**: Augusto B. Corrêa, André G. Pereira, Jendrik Seipp
- **Abstract**: In recent years, large language models (LLMs) have shown remarkable capabilities in various artificial intelligence problems. However, they fail to plan reliably, even when prompted with a detailed definition of the planning task. Attempts to improve their planning capabilities, such as chain-of-thought prompting, fine-tuning, and explicit "reasoning" still yield incorrect plans and usually fail to generalize to larger tasks. In this paper, we show how to use LLMs to generate correct plans, even for out-of-distribution tasks of increasing size. For a given planning domain, we ask an LLM to generate several domain-dependent heuristic functions in the form of Python code, evaluate them on a set of training tasks within a greedy best-first search, and choose the strongest one. The resulting LLM-generated heuristics solve many more unseen test tasks than state-of-the-art domain-independent heuristics for classical planning. They are even competitive with the strongest learning algorithm for domain-dependent planning. These findings are especially remarkable given that our proof-of-concept implementation is based on an unoptimized Python planner and the baselines all build upon highly optimized C++ code. In some domains, the LLM-generated heuristics expand fewer states than the baselines, revealing that they are not only efficiently computable, but sometimes even more informative than the state-of-the-art heuristics. Overall, our results show that sampling a set of planning heuristic function programs can significantly improve the planning capabilities of LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate domain-dependent heuristic functions for classical planning. The approach involves prompting an LLM with a PDDL domain description, examples from other domains, and planner API details. The LLM then generates multiple candidate heuristics as Python code. These heuristics are evaluated on a set of training tasks using a greedy best-first search (GBFS) planner, and the best-performing heuristic is selected for unseen test tasks.  The results show that LLM-generated heuristics outperform domain-independent heuristics like hFF and are even competitive with the state-of-the-art learning algorithm for domain-dependent planning, especially given the Pyperplan planner used is in Python while baselines use highly optimized C++ code. The paper claims that LLM-generated heuristics are not only efficiently computable but also, in some cases, more informative than state-of-the-art heuristics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to generate heuristic functions isn't entirely novel. Tuisov et al. [80] have explored this direction. However, the paper presents a significantly streamlined and more effective approach compared to prior work. A key difference is the generation of a constant set of heuristics per domain which is then selected for use across new tasks. This amortizes the cost of LLM inference. Also, the authors directly generate code usable in an off-the-shelf planner, whereas other approaches require some adaptation and manual involvement. The empirical focus on outperforming *existing* heuristics and not just generating code is important. The fact that it can actually improve upon the best existing hand coded heuristics for classical planning is novel.

*   **Significance:** The results are surprisingly significant. The fact that an LLM-generated heuristic, implemented in a slower Python planner (Pyperplan), can outperform highly optimized C++ planners using standard heuristics (like hFF) in terms of coverage and, in some cases, even state expansions, is a notable achievement. The implication is that LLMs can encode domain-specific knowledge effectively, even from PDDL descriptions, and create heuristics that guide search more efficiently. These findings challenge the conventional wisdom in classical planning. The results demonstrate LLMs can automate what was previously a manual process of domain expert involvement.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and approach
    *   Well-defined experimental setup using IPC domains
    *   Strong empirical evidence supporting the claims
    *   A comparison to state-of-the-art approaches is included
    *   The streamlined approach for generating the heuristic (direct code injection)

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on a single planner (Pyperplan) limits generalizability. While the results are impressive *given* this limitation, it also raises questions about whether the LLM-generated heuristics are particularly well-suited to Pyperplan's internal workings or genuinely superior across different planning architectures.
    *   The memory requirements during the training phase are not strictly enforced.
    *   The paper mainly focuses on coverage and expansion counts. More detailed analysis of plan quality (e.g., plan length) and runtime characteristics across tasks would strengthen the findings.
    *   The reliance on temperature parameter tuning requires some manual effort
    *   The cost of the LLM model can be high

*   **Impact:** The paper could have a significant impact on the field by encouraging more research into using LLMs for automated heuristic design. It could also inspire new planning architectures that are better suited to leveraging LLM-generated knowledge. The authors have demonstrated the potential for LLMs to replace human expertise in designing domain-specific heuristics, reducing costs and increasing accessibility to planning.

*   **Score Justification:**
    Despite the impressive results, the limitations mentioned above prevent a top score. While the results show significant gains when used with a slower Python-based planner, whether this translates directly to C++ implementations is not verified, even though it can be seen as implied.  The strong dependence on a single planner (Pyperplan) is also a major concern. While it challenges the state-of-the-art and potentially enables new research directions, the results are confined to a relatively educational platform.
